ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܒܢܫ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܟܕ ܪ̈ܚܝܩܢ. ܚܕ ܟܡܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܒܩܪܝܒܘܬܐ. ܐܦܢ ܬܘܒ ܙܗܝܪܐܝܬ ܒܬܪ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܢܢ. ܐܠܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܢܫܢܐ ܠܡܕܒܪܐ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܿܢ ܗܢܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܢܝܼܢ ܛܘܒܢ̈ܐ ܒܪܘܬܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܝܩܝܪܘܬܗ̇ ܕܩܕܝܫܬܐ. ܗܼܝ ܐܒܗܠܝܢ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܠܠܬ. ܘܟܕ ܫܡܥܘ ܐܘܕܝܘ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܘܩܒܠܘ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܕܢܒܢܘܢ ܘܢܛܝܒܘܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܒܥܘܕܐ ܕܩܪܝܬܐ ܙܥܘܪܬܐ. ܟܕ ܗܼܝ ܐܠܨܬ ܬܘܒ ܕܬܬܓܘܐ ܡܛܝܒܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܗܼܢܘܢ ܩܡܘ ܘܥܨܐܘܗ̇ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪܝܢ. ܕܠܟܝ ܐܡܐ ܒܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܟܕ ܬܥܡܪܝܢ ܒܪܘܚ. ܡܕܒܪܐ ܗܝ ܠܟܝ. ܐܠܐ ܚܘܪܝ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܢܦܫ̈ܬܐ ܕܬܠܝܢ ܐܒܟܝ. ܗܝܕܝܢ ܐܬܬܛܦܝܣܬ ܠܗܘܢ. ܘܒܢܝܼܘ ܠܗܝܢ ܕܝܪܐ ܚܒܝܫܬܐ ܘܡܓܕܠܐ ܒܓܘܗ̇. ܘܥܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܗܿ. ܗܿܝ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ. ܓܢܒܪܘܬܐ ܒܝܕ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܥܠ ܫܝ̈ܕܐ ܫܩܠܬ. ܟܕ ܡܟܝܠ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܐܝܟ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܒ̈ܢܝܢܫܐ ܠܩܘܒܠܗ̇ ܐܪܒܐ ܣܕܝܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܒܙܝܢܐ ܥܠܝܗ ܐܬܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܟܠ ܫܘܚܠܦܝܢ. ܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ
upon saints is generally made through women even when they are absent, how much more therefore in their presence!), even if so be we continue to run with caution after spiritual things. But now let us withdraw into the desert beyond this". While the blessed men were ashamed on account of the saint's serious character, she herself addressed them in these words; and when they heard it they gave thanks to God; and they undertook to build and prepare for them a place in the vicinity of that village, while she herself intended to move farther up country; and they stood up and opposed her, saying: "For you, mother, in a city, if you dwell in it, it is a desert to you but look at these souls who are hanging [upon you Then she gave way to them: and they built an enclosed monastery for them and a tower inside it; and they took up their abode in it. But the blessed woman through strength proceeding from Grace received mighty power over fiends, since they would thenceforth set themselves in array against her night and day in the form (τύπτος) of human beings, and they would come against her with all varieties of weapons; and she like a strong man who


[p. 554] {#po.18.p554}
ܚܫܠܬܢܐ ܕܡܐܛ ܠܟܪ̈ܝܗܐ ܕܓܙܡܝܢ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܒܗܘܢ ܡܪܚܠܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܛܒܐ ܕܚܝܠܬܢܘܬܗ̇ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܡܫܬܡܥ ܗܘܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܢ ܛܒܐ ܕܓܡܝܪܘܬܗ̇ ܠܚܙܬܗ̇. ܥܡ ܐܕܗܠܝܢ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܕܬܡܢ: ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܦܢ̈ܫܐ ܚܡܫܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܗܽܘ ܗܘܬ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܐܫܬܘܝܢܢ ܠܚܙܬܗ̇ ܕܗܺܝ ܕܝܩܝܪ ܥܠܝ ܕܐܢܬܬܐ ܐܫܡܗ ܬܗܿܪܬ ܒܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܚܫܐ ܘܕܚܟܡܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܦܩ̈ܝܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܡܢ ܦܘܡܗܿ. ܟܕ ܝܘ̈ܡܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘ̇ܝܬ ܠܘܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܩܕ̈ܝܫܐ. ܟܕ ܙܠ ܐܢܐ ܘܐܬܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܫܡܥ ܡܠܬܗ̇ ܘܥܢܝܢܗ̇. ܒܣܝܡܐ. ܘܠܐ ܐܦܟܚܬ ܐܚܝܿܙܐ ܪܝܫܗ̇ ܕܬܪܝܨ ܠܥܠ. ܐܘ ܐܬܐ ܡܢ ܦܪܨܘܦܗܿ. ܐܠܐ ܒܿܠܚܘܕ ܪܝܫ ܢܚܝܪܗܿ. ܕܗܟܢܐ ܚܫܝܫܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܒܟܝܐ. ܘܦܩܕܬ ܕܬܐܡܪ ܠܘܬܢ ܥܠ ܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܚܝܕ ܒܛܥܝܘܬܐ. ܘܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܚܙܝܢ ܒܢܝܢ̈ܫܐ ܠܚܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܡܬܡܣܝܢ ܘܡܬܚܬܒܠܝܢ ܘܡܪܚܠܝܢ ܒܓܘ ܩܒܪ̈ܐ: ܘܪܚܫܐ ܣܢܝܐ ܕܡܓܝܓ ܘܚܠܝܛ ܒܪܚܠܗܿܘܢ. ܘܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܚܙܝܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܛܥܝܢ ܒܣܪ̈ܝܩܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܓܝܿܠܐ. ܘܐܝܟܢܐ ܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܣܠܩ ܥܠ ܠܒܗܘܢ ܕܒܢ̈ܝܢܫܐ: ܗܽܘ ܥܕܢܐ
despises the sickly men who threaten him would mock at them And the fame of her strength was heard everywhere, so that the fame of her perfection instigated us also to see her as well as the holy men who were there, fifteen years after she had settled in the same desert And, when we had been privileged to see her that | do not like to name a woman, | was amazed at the words of solemn feeling and of the wisdom of God that proceeded from her mouth during the space of more than twenty days that | was with these saints, going and coming, in order to hear her speech and her delightful conversation; and | was unable to see her head held erect, or a feature of her face (πρόσωπον) except the tip of her nose only; so sorrowful was she and tearful. is in the grasp of error, and how men see their fellows rotting and decaying and putrefying within graves, and the loathsome worm creeping about in the midst of their putrefying remains, and they who see these things are led astray by the vanities of this false world, and how again the thought of the rapidly-approaching terrible hour of the dissolution of this world does And she took upon herself to speak to us about this world how it


[p. 555] {#po.18.p555}
ܪܗܝܒܐ ܘܕܚܝܠܐ ܕܫܪܝܢ ܒܠܒܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܒܢܝܢܫܐ. ܘܕܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܠܡܩܡ ܩܕܡ ܒܝܡܐ ܕܚܝܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܟܕ ܐܡܪܐ. ܕܓܘܚܟܐ ܗܘ ܪܒܐ ܕܫܡܥܝܢܢ ܦܚܬ ܕܢܘܪܐ: ܘܬܗܘ̈ܡܐ ܕܦܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ: ܘܚܫܘܟܐ ܘܬܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܬܫܢܝܩܐ: ܘܠܐ ܡܬܝܢܢ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ܇. ܘܫܐܠܝܢܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܥܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܐܬܪܐ: ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܢܫܬܘܙܒ. ܘܕܕܚܝܠܢ ܬܘܒ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܣܓܝܐܬܐ܂. ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܢ ܬܢܚܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܠܕܘܡܪܐ ܪܒܐ ܥܠ ܡܠܬܗ̇ ܫܪܟܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܐܬܝܗܒܬ ܠܗ̇ ܐܦ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܐܣܝܘܬܐ.. ܕܟܠ ܟܐܒܐ ܐܘ ܡܚܘܬܐ ܕܬܬܛܠܩ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܐܝܕܗ̇ ܬܬܐܣܐ'. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܐܬܡܢ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ". ܐܢܫ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܪܚܡܐܠܗܐ' ܕܐܠܗܐ ܥܡܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܕܘܟܬܐ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ.. ܕܠܘ ܣܓܝ ܦܪܝܩܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢܗܘܢ. ܘܥܡܗ ܣܕܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܫܝ̈ܐܕܐ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ. ܘܚܙܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܬܟܬܘܫܗܘܢ ܕܥܡ ܛܘܒܢܝܬܐܼ ܘܗܝ ܠܕܝܠܗ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܝ ܚܝܠܬܢܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢܗ: ܘܡܫܠܛܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܠܐ ܕܚܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܣܟ ܡܢ ܫܝ̈ܐܕܐ: ܘܗܘܬ ܠܗ̇ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܐܕܡܢܛܐ ܡܫܪܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܬܙܝܥܢܐ". ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܫܝ̈ܐܕܐ ܠܘܬܗܿ ܩܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܗܕܐ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܗܝ܂ ܗܘܬ ܠܗ̇ ܕܝܢ ܟܐܦܐ. ܘܡܢ ܒܣܪܐ ܦܪܙܠܐ. ܗܠܝܢ
not rise in men's hearts, and that they are about to stand before the terrible judgment-seat (βῆμα) of God, saying, "It is a great absurdity that we hear of the pit of fire, and the abysses of flame, and the darkness, and the rest of the torments, and we do not lay it to heart and ask mercy while we have the opportunity as a means of escaping from these things." This divine woman would repeat in our presence many things yet more terrible than these, and we were brought into a state of great wonder at her speech. Then there was given to her also the gift of healing, so that every infirmity or disease upon which she stretched out her hand was healed While we were there, a certain great and God-loving blessed man was living ina place in the desert that was not far from them; and the fiends used openly to set themselves in array against him; and he used to see their struggle with the blessed woman, and she his. But, since she was stronger than he, she both prevailed and had no fear whatever of the fiends, and she had become firm and immoveable like adamant, insomuch that the demons themselves used to cry to her, "This is a woman, but she has become stone, and from flesh iron". These things the demons used to say to her. And,


[p. 556] {#po.18.p556}
ܗܢܘܢ ܕܝܘ̈ܐ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܘܬܗ̇. ܘܐܚܪ ܡܢ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܡܫܪܪܐ ܗܘܬ ..” ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܘ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܬܩܦܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ.. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܝܠܬܢܝܬܐ ܠܘܬܗ̇ ܐܬܐ ܗܘܐ. ܘܟܕ ܬܡܢ ܐܝܬܝ ܐܬܝ ܟܕ ܦܩܝܕ ܘܒܠܝܥ ܒܪܥܝܢܗ ܘܡܕܘܕ. ܘܟܕ ܚܙܬܗ ܐܣܬܟܠܬ. ܘܫܐܠܬܗ ܕܡܢܐ ܫܚܝܩ ܐܢܬ ܐܒܐ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܕܚܣܢܘܢ ܫܝܐܕܐ ܘܢܩܠܘ ܗܘܢܝ: ܟܕ ܗܐ ܟܠܗ ܫܒܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܪܡܝܢܢ ܒܬܟܬܘܫܐ. ܘܒܗܢܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܘܐܝܡܡܐ: ܚܙܘ̈ܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܠܐ ܕܪܗܝܒܘܬܐ ܚܘܝܘ ܠܝ ܂. ܘܐܬܬܙܝܥ ܠܒܝ ܘܐܫܬܩܠ ܗܘܢܝ ܘܐܬܕܘܕܬ. ܘܡܢ ܕܚܠܬܐ ܥܪܩܝܬ. ܘܡܣܬܪܕ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܥܘܠ ܠܩܠܝܬܝ ܡܢ ܚ̈ܙܘܐ ܕܚܙܝܬ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܫܒܩ ܐܢܐ ܠܗܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܗ̇ܝ"'. ܗܝ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܫܡܥܬ. ܐܢܝܕܬ ܒܪܝܫܗ̇ ܟܕ ܐܡܪܐ. ܬܘ ܚܙܘ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܡܬܩܪܝܢ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ. ܘܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܐܠܐ ܘܥܒܕ̈ܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ. ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܕܥܒܕ̈ܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܬܬܩܪܘܢ. ܗܟܢܐ ܚܫܒܬܝܗܝ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܪܟܘܢ ܡܚܝܠܐ. ܘܚܘܝܬܘܢ ܒܝܕ ܪܦܫܘܬܟܘܢ ܕܝܥܠܟܘܢ ܥܠ ܚܝܠܗ ܬܩܦܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܙܕܟܝܢܐ. ܗܘ ܕܣܝܚܦ ܘܕܐܟ ܘܒܛܠ ܠܟܠܗ ܚܝܠܗ ܕܣܛܢܐ: ܕܒܨܝܪ ܗܘ ܡܢ ܕܫܝ̈ܐܕܐ"..
inasmuch as she was so firm, that blessed man, whenever the visions pressed hard upon him, used to come to her as to a strong person; and while | was there, he came, stricken and smitten in his mind and perturbed. And when she saw him she understood; and she asked him "Why are you distressed, father?" But he continued saying: "The fiends have been mighty and have taken away my wits, since the whole week we have been more than ordinarily involved in a struggle; and this night and day they showed me terrible sights of fear, and my heart was shaken and my wits were taken away and | was confounded; and from fear | fled; and | am afraid to enter my cell on account of the sights that | saw. And now | will leave that place". She on hearing it shook her head, saying: "Come see persons that are called men, and not only men but also Christ's bondmen. Is it right that you should be called Christ's bondmen? You have in this way reckoned Christ your Lord to be feeble, and by your own frailty have made the mighty and invincible power of Christ, which cast down and overthrew and brought to naught all the power of Satan, appear less than that of the fiends, so that Christ now seems to be a runaway and one that


[p. 557] {#po.18.p557}
ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܣܬܒܪ ܗܫܐ ܡܫܝܚܐ: ܥܪܘܩܐ! ܘܡܙܕܟܝܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܕܝܘ̈ܐ. ܗܢܘܢ ܢܫܬܟܚܘܢ ܚܣ̈ܝܢܐ ܘܙܟܝ̈ܝ ܠܚܝܠܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ .° ܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܬܠܡܝܕܗ ܕܗܘ ܕܐܡܪ. ܕܬܕܘܟܘܢ ܠܟܠܗ ܚܝܠܗ ܕܒܥܠܕܒܒܐ. ܫܒܘܩ ܠܝ ܐܒܘܢ. ܕܐܥܫܩܬܢܝ܀ ܕܚܕܝܬ ܠܫܝ̈ܐܕܐܼ. ܐܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܡܬܒܥܝܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܡܛܠ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ% ܒܗܢܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܫܡܥ ܗܘܝܬ ܕܡܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܩܠܬܐ ܕܬܟܬܘ̈ܫܝܗܘܢ ܕܕ̈ܝܘܐ ܕܚܘܝܘ ܠܝ ܐܡܗ ܗܘܝܬ. ܘܐܢܐ ܝܕܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ' ܚܕܝܪ ܠܡܚܝܠܘܬܝ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܫܘܪܐ ܕܢܚܫܐ. ܘܚܝܠܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܕܢܩܘܡ ܠܩܘܒܠܗ ܠܝܬ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܒܫܒܐܝܬ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܚܝܠܬܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ܢ ܐܡܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܚܫܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ.. ܟܕ ܗܝ ܐܚܕ ܡܢܢ ܠܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܐ"!. ܗܝܕܝܢ ܐܡܪܬ ܬܟܝܠܐܝܬ ܠܛܘܒܢܝܐ. ܥܠ ܫܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܩܘܡ ܗܦܘܟ ܠܩܠܝܬܟ. ܘܗܘ ܡܫܦܠ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܥܢܕ ܠܟܠܗ ܚܝܠܗ ܕܒܝܫܐ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܟܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܡܠܬܗ̇ ܕܐܢܬܬܐ: ܟܕ ܛܒ ܐܬܣܪܕ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܟܘܚܕܗ
can be overcome by the demons, and they are found to be strong and vanquishers of the power of God. How are you a disciple of him who said. ‘Ye shall trample on all the power of the enemy’ Forgive me, our father. You have distressed me by causing the fiends to rejoice. [lf necessary, were it not for other reasons you should only last night have been hearing the shapes and voices of the struggles of the demons which they showed me, and [have been marvelling at them. And for my part | know that the Lord's power surrounds my weakness like a wall of bronze, and there is no other power that can resist it". While the weak woman was boldly saying these words in our presence, we looked at one another, while she did not see either of us. Then she said confidently to the blessed man: "In the name of the Lord Jesus Christ rise and return to your cell; and he as his custom is will make all the power of the evil one weak before you". But the blessed man, being struck with shame at the woman's words and trembling greatly from his shame, consented to go. And, when


[p. 558] {#po.18.p558}
ܘܟܕ ܩܡܢܢ ܡܢ ܠܘܐܗ̇ ܘܡܡܠܠܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܘܬܗܝܪܝܢ ܒܚܝܠܗ̇ ܪܘܚܢܐ ܘܒܠܒܝܒܘܬܗܿ. ܐܡܿܪܬ ܠܗ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܐ ܦܪܨܘܦܗ ܕܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܗܕܐ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܒܚܕ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܕ ܡܿܡܠܠܝܢܢ ܐܡܪܬ ܠܗܿ. ܕܡܢܘ ܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܡܚܦܝܬܝ ܐܦܝ̈ܟܝ. ܘܚܡܬ ܠܝ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪܐ. ܕܗܘ ܕܣܡ ܥܠܝ ܢܝܪܗ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܝܕܥ ܐܚܘܢ. ܕܡܢ ܕܩܒܝܠܬ ܢܝܪܗ ܗܐ ܝܘܡܢܐ ܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܚܡܫ ܫܢ̈ܝܢ. ܦܪܨܘܦܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ ܠܐ ܚܿܙܝܬ. ܘܕܝܠܟ ܗܟܢܐ ܒܝܿܥܐ ܐܢ݇ܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܚܿܙܐ. ܘܐܢܐ ܠܡ ܐܡܪܬ ܠܗ̇. ܕܡܛܠ ܡܪܢ ܐܡܼܪܝ ܠܝ ܫܪܪܐ. ܕܐܢܬܝ ܕܚܠܐ ܐܢܬܝ ܕܠܐ ܬܚܣܪܝܢ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ. ܐܘ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܐ ܢܚܣܪ ܒܚܙܬܟܝ. ܗܼܝ ܕܝܢ ܐܸܡܪ̇ܬ, ܕܡܛܠ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܟܕ ܬܗܝܪܝܢܢ ܒܕܘܒܪ̈ܝܗ̇ ܕܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܘܒܡܝܬܪܘܬܗ̇ ܕܒܬܘܠܝܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܫܘܫܢ. ܫܢܝܢܢ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܟܕ
we had risen up from before her and were speaking and expressing astonishment at her spiritual strength and her courage, | said to him: "How is it that this blessed woman's face (πρόσωπον) is not visible?" He on his side said to me: "One day while we were speaking | said to her: ‘For what reason do you hide your face from us?' And she made oath to me, saying: ‘He who by his grace laid his yoke upon me knows, our brother, that since | took his yoke upon me, which is to day twenty-five years ago, | have not seen a man's face (πρόσωπον), and do you desire me to see yours now?’, and | on my side said to her: 'For our Lord's sake tell me the truth. Are you for your part afraid lest you should yourself suffer harm from the sight of a man, or lest he should suffer harm from the sight of you?' And she said 'For both reasons'." And thus in astonishment at the saint's habits of life and the endurance of Susan the virgin of Christ we departed from that place, praising God.
ܡܫܒܚܝܢܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ܀ ܫܼܠܡܬ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܒܬܘܠܬܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܫܡܗ̇ ܫܘܫܢ܀
[The history of a blessed virgin of Christ whose name was Susan is ended.


[p. 559] {#po.18.p559}

### Next the twenty-eighth history, of the blessed Mary the anchorite
ܬܘܒ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܥܣܪܝܢ ܘܬܡܢܐ ܕܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܡܪܝܡ ܐܒܝܠܬܐ܀ ܐܦܠܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܒܨܝܪܐ ܡܢ ܬܗܪܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܡܪܝܡ. ܕܒܟܝܢܐ ܒܠܚܘܕܐ ܕܢܩܒܬܐ ܫܩܝܠܐ. ܗܘܬ ܕܢܩ̈ܒܬܐ. ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܢ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܘܨܒܝܢܐ. ܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܫܚ̈ܝܡܐ. ܐܠܐ ܕܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܓܢܒܪ̈ܐ ܘܚܝ̈ܠܬܢܐ ܦܩܝܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܘܐܦ ܗܼܝ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ̇܀ ܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܗܕܐ. ܒܪܬ ܐܢܫܐ ܝܕܝ̈ܥܐ ܘܓܢܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܒܥܠܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܐܦ ܗܼܝ. ܓܒܪܐ ܕܝܢ ܣܒܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܒܩܪܝܬܐ ܚܕܐ ܒܬܚ̈ܘܡܐ ܕܩܪܝܬܗܘܢ ܚܒܝܫ ܗܘܐ. ܥܬܝ̇ܩܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܢ̈ܝܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ. ܘܠܘܬ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܗܢܐ ܐܬܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܗ̇ ܕܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ: ܟܕ ܗܼܝ. ܥܡܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܒܪܬ ܫܢ̈ܝܐ ܥܣܪ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܼܘܬ. ܘܟܕ ܠܝܩܝܪܘܬܗ ܕܣܝܒܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܠܝܡܬܐ. ܚܢܐ ܒܬܪܥܝܬܗ̇ ܕܐܬܠ ܗܘܐ. ܘܠܥܠܡܐ ܟܠܗ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܕܒܝܗ ܒܝ̇ܣܪܐ ܗܘܬ. ܟܕ
Therefore neither was the history of this holy Mary unworthy of admiration, a woman who by nature only bore the form of females, but herself also too bore in herself the character and soul and will not only of ordinary men, but of mighty and valiant men. This blessed woman therefore also was the daughter of distinguished persons and a high family (γένος) in the world. Now there was a great old man of God who was living as a recluse in a village bordering on their village, aman far advanced in years; and to this saint the blessed woman's people used frequently to come, she being with them, when she was ten years old. And, when the damsel saw the serious character of the old man, solemn feeling was stirred in her mind, and she despised all the world with all that is in it, meditating in herself and saying: "Lo! they are rich who


[p. 560] {#po.18.p560}
ܡܬܚܫܒܐ ܒܢܦܫܗ̇ ܘܐܡܿܪܐ. ܕܗܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܥܬܝܪ̈ܐ. ܕܩܢܝܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܒܪ ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ. ܠܘ ܚܢܢ ܕܘ̈ܝܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܥܼܬܪ ܐܢܫ ܘܐܬܚܬܪ ܐܦ ܐܫܬܒܗܪ. ܘܐܣܝܼܒܪ ܕܗܘܝܘ ܡܪܐ ܕܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܝܠܗ. ܐܬܐ ܡܘܬܐ ܘܠܓܢܝܗܝ ܒܫܝܘܠ. ܘܐܪܚܡܝܘܢ ܐܚܖ̈ܢܐ ܠܐܘܬܗ ܕܡܢ ܛܠܝܘܬܗ ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܡܡܘܬܗ. ܘܓܕܫܐ ܕܒܚܛܝܬܐ ܡܕܟܢܫ. ܘܦܝܼܫܬ ܚܛܝܬܐ ܠܗ. ܘܗܘܼܐ ܩܢܝܢܗ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܗܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܠܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ. ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ. ܘܣܪܝܩܘܬܐ. ܡܿܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܐ ܫܒܩܝܢܢ ܠܗܼ ܒܨܒܝܢܢ. ܠܗܽܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܢ ܟܠܦܪܘܣ ܐܦܢ ܠܐ ܢܨܒܐ. ܕܢܫܒܩܗ ܐܝܬ ܠܢ. ܘܡܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܠܝܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܗܢܐ ܣܒܐ ܠܐ ܗܽܘܝܢܢ. ܠܘ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ. ܚܣ ܠܝ ܡܟܝܠ ܕܢܬܛܥܐ ܒܝ ܥܠܝܡܝܐ. ܘܐܦܘܩ ܡܢ ܪܗܼܛܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܣܒܐ ܗܢܐ. ܘܡܢ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܪܬܚ ܪܥܝܢܗ̇ ܕܥܠܝܡܬܐ. ܘܦܣܩܬ ܢܦܫܗ̇ ܡܢ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ ܘܡܢ ܡܫܬܝܐ ܕܚܡܪܐ ܓܡܝܪܐܝܬ. ܘܫܪܝܬ ܒܨܘܡܐ ܘܒܨܠܘܬܐ. ܘܒܪܗܛܐ ܕܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܡܝܬܪܘܬܐ. ܐܢܫ̈ܝܗ̇ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܚܙܘ ܗܠܝܢ. ܟܐܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܗ̇. ܘܠܨܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܕܬܬܚܫܚ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ ܘܡܫܬܝܐ ܕܚܡܪܐ. ܘܗܼܝ ܠܐ ܡܬܛܦܝܣܐ ܗܘܬ. ܟܕ ܫܕܐ ܠܒܣܬܪ ܓܘܫܡܗܿ.
possess the world that passes not away for ever and ever, not we wretched persons among whom, as soon as a man has become rich and proud and boastful, and believed himself to be lord of his own riches, death has come and cast him down into sheol, and others have taken away his toil in which he toiled from his childhood even to his death, and which has perhaps been amassed by sin, and the sin has remained with him, and the riches have become the property of others. Lo! then the things that are in this world are all deceit and vanity. For what reason do we not voluntarily leave a thing which we must in any case leave even if we do not desire it? and for what reason do we not become as this old man? [Is not this a human being? Far be it from me henceforth to let the world deceive me, and refrain from following the course of life of this old man". And from that time the damsel's mind became inflamed, and she cut herself off completely from eating flesh and from drinking wine; and she began to fast and pray, and to run in the road of excellence. But her people, when they saw these things, used to reprove her, and to press her to continue eating flesh and drinking wine; and she would not obey, having cast her body, and all the enticements of youth, and the adornment of childhood behind her, and begun to walk


[p. 561] {#po.18.p561}
ܘܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܘܬܚܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܥܠܝܡܘܬܐ. ܘܣܝܩܠܐ ܕܛܠܝܘܬܐ. ܘܦܪܝܬ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܪܡܬܐ ܕܐܒܝܠܘܬܐ ܘܕܥܢܘܝܘܬܐ. ܕܚܠܝܨܐܝܬ ܬܪܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܡܟܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܐܢܫ ܥܬܝܪܐ. ܪܝܫܘ ܐܚܡ ܒ̈ܢܝ ܥܠܡܐ ܡܬܬܥܝܩܝܢ ܘܡܬܚܫܒܝܢ: ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܘ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܒܠܚܘܕܐ: ܐܠܐ ܘܐܦ ܕܗܘ ܣܢܐ ܫܦܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ: ܕܢܩܘܡ ܠܡ ܢܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܡܫܬܘܬܐ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܬܡܪܕ ܠܗ ܘܬܐܙܠ ܠܕܝܪܐ. ܘܢܘܒܕ ܠܗܘ ܕܡܬܛܝܒ ܕܢܣܒܝܘܗܝ. ܘܟܕ ܗܢܘܢ ܫܪܝܘ ܡܛܝܒܝܢ ܘܡܥܬܕ̈ܝܢ ܛܘܝ̈ܒܐ ܕܡܫܬܘܬܐ. ܪܓܝܫܬ ܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܐܡܪܬ. ܠܐ ܬܠܐܘܢ ܣܪܝܩܐܝܬ ܘܬܚܣܪܘܢ. ܕܐܢܐ ܡܣܝܒܪܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܙܘܘܓܐ ܕܢ ܠܕܟ̈ܝܐ ܘܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܘܒ̈ܬܘܠܐ: ܠܣܡܟܐ ܕܡܫܬܘܬܗ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܦܛܪܐ. ܕܠܐ ܗܘܝܐ ܠܝ ܦܘܬܦܘܬܐ ܦܓܪܢܝܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܚܒܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܝ. ܒܢܩܦܐ ܒܣܪܢܝܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ ܡܝܘܬܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܕܐ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܚܦܛܐ ܗܘܬ: ܐܚܟ ܕܢܟܢܫܘܢ ܕܢܫܥܒܕܘܢܗ̇. ܫܕܒܩܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܐܙܠܬ ܠܕܝܪܐ ܚܕܐ. ܘܣܦܪܬ ܪܝܫܗ̇ ܘܢܣܒܬ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ. ܘܡܢ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܫܩܠܬ ܠܗ̇ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܒܟܠܡܕܡ. ܠܣܒܐ ܗܘ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܒܩܘܡܐ ܘܒܢܙܝܪܘܬܐ ܘܒܒܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܒܬܠܝܬܢܐ. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܟܘܬܝܢܐ ܕܣܥܪܐ ܘܣܢܕܠܐ ܠܒܫܬ
soundly in the lofty road of anchoritism and asceticism. Since she had been betrothed by them to a certain rich man, they began as people of the world to be distressed and to think thoughts which were not theirs only but those of the hater of good things also as well, "Let us rise and make a weddingfeast for her, lest she run away and go to a monastery, and we lose the man who was to take her to wife". And, when they on their side had begun to prepare and make ready the things required for the wedding, the blessed woman became aware of it and said to them: "Do not toil to no purpose and lose your money; for my part | hope in him who invited the pure and holy and virgin to recline at his eternal wedding-feast that ceases not that | shall make no bodily partnership, nor corrupt the purity of my soul by fleshly conjunction with a mortal man". But, when this incited these people all the more, so that they met together (7) in order to constrain her, she left them and went to a monastery; and she shaved her head and took the habit (σχῆμα). And from that time she took that holy old man as her model (τύπτος) in all things, in standing and abstinence and vigils and eating once in three days, while she also made for herself a hair-shirt and sandals (σανδάλια), and


[p. 562] {#po.18.p562}
ܠܗ̇. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܣܥܪܗ̇ ܡܢ ܝܘܡܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܩܒܠܬ ܪܘܫܡܐ. ܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܣܦܪܬܗ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܒܦܢܝ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܠܥܠ ܘܠܬܚܬ ܥܦܫܦ ܗܘܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܒܚܪܬܐ ܠܡ ܠܝܩܝܪܘܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܗܘܐ. ܘܐܦ ܒܐܓܘܢܐ ܕܪܕܝܦܝܘܬܐ ܫܢܝܐ ܚܡܫܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܐܬܢܨܚܬ. ܟܕ ܠܘ ܒܝܘ̈ܡܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܢܦܝܩܐ ܗܘܬ. ܐܠܐ ܘܫܒ̈ܥܐ. ܘܥܣܪܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ. ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܛܥܡܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܕܡ. ܘܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܚܝܠܬܢܐܝܬ ܘܓܢܒܪܐܝܬ ܫܢܝܐ ܬܠܬܝܢ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܪܕܬ. ܫܠܡܬ. ܪܗܛܝܗ̇ ܘܩܒܠܬ ܟܠܝܠܐ
moreover from the first day on which she received the mark never again cut her hair, so that it became long and abundant, until it was wound round her four times up and down, so that at last (it is said) Tit became a great weight. And she also distinguished herself in the conflict (ἀγών) of persecution for fifteen years, no longer passing night-vigils, but vigils lasting even a week, and ten days, and then she would taste something. And, when she had walked strongly and heroically in the road of righteousness for thirty years, she finished her course and received the crown earned by her life, and fell asleep in peace.
ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܢ. ܘܐܬܬܢܝܚܬ ܒܫܠܡܐ. ܫܠܡܬ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܛܘܒܢܝܬܐ ܡܪܝܡ ܐܒܝܠܬܐ
[The history of the blessed Mary the anchorite is ended.

### The twenty-ninth(?) history, of Malkha the mendicant and stranger
ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܟܣܪܝܢ ܘܬܫܥܐ ܕܡܠܟܐ ܡܣܪܩܐ ܘܐܟܣܢܝܐ. ܒܙܒܢ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܒܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܕܥܠ ܓܢܒ ܐܡܕ ܡܕܝܢܬܐ: ܩܕܡ ܕܢܬܪܕܦ ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܛܝܩܘ: ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܬܪܝ ܐܚܪܝ. ܨܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬܢ ܐܢܫ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ
Once when we were in our convent adjacent to the city of Amida before it was driven into banishment by the heretics, at the time of the second


[p. 563] {#po.18.p563}
ܕܠܒܝܫ ܗܘܐ ܡܐܢ ܨܐܐ ܘܕܒܝ̈ܠܝܢ ܛܒ. ܘܟܕ ܐܬܩܒܠ ܘܐܬܬܒܥ ܕܢܚܪܡ ܗܪܣܝܣ ܗܝ ܕܒܗܘ ܙܒܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܥܝܕܐ ܠܡܬܒܥ. ܩܡ ܗܘܐ ܥܡܢ ܒܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܪܡܫܐ ܘܒܦܬܘܪܐ ܟܕ ܫܠܐ ܘܦܫܝܩ. ܘܚܝܪ ܚܕܐ ܚܕܐ ܡܓܢܒܐܝܬ ܒܫܡܝܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܟܕ ܒܪܚܡܬ ܐܟܣܢܝܘܬܐ ܐܬܩܒܠ ܘܐܬܬܢܝܚܬ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܫܬܝܩ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܡܡܠܠܐ. ܘܡܠܐ ܦܘܡܗ ܒܬܘܕܝܬܐ. ܐܢܫܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܪܝ: ܟܕ ܚܙܘ ܐܣܟܝܡܗ ܐܦ ܪܟܝܢܘܬܗ ܘܪܡܙܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܗ. ܐܫܬܘܕܥܘ ܬܪܥܝܬܗ ܘܐܡܪܘ ܗܘܘ ܠܥܪ̈ܟܐ. ܕܓܒܪܐ ܗܢܐ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ. ܦܠܚܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܒܫܪܪܐ. ܘܩܪܒ ܗܘ ܗܘ ܕܐܡܪ ܘܡܫܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ. ܘܐܡܪ. ܕܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܩܕܝܫܘܬܟ ܐܒܐ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܬܚܬ ܚܝܪ ܗܘܐ. ܘܡܬܬܢܚ. ܐܡܪ ܠܗ. ܕܫܒܘܩ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ. ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܕܘܝܐ ܐܝܬܝ. ܐܡܪ ܠܗ. ܟܠܢ ܐܒܐ ܐܟܣ̈ܢܝܐ ܚܢܢ. ܘܬܘܬܒܐ. ܐܠܐ ܨܒܝܢܢ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܕܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܪ. ܡܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܠܨܐ ܥܠ ܥܘܩܒܐ. ܡܐ ܕܒܪ ܐܕܡ ܚܙܝܬ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ: ܕܥ
Theshri a certain stranger arrived among us, clad in dirty and much-worn raiment; and, when he had been received and been required to anathematize the heresy that prevailed at that time, as it was the custom to require, he stood with us at vespers and at table in silence and without speaking, now and again looking furtively to heaven. And so, when he had been hospitably received and his soul had been refreshed, he simply refrained from conversation and filled his mouth with thanksgiving. But some of the houseworkers (?) when they saw his habit (σχῆμα) and his downward looks and the indication provided by his practices, recognised his character and said to the rest, "This stranger is in truth a servant of God". And the man who said it himself came up and continued asking him and saying: "Whence comes your holiness, father?". But he continued looking downward and sighing. He says to him: "Forgive me, sir. | am a miserable stranger". He says to him: "All of us, father, are strangers, and sojourners But we wish to learn whence you come". But he on his side


[p. 564] {#po.18.p564}
ܕܥܦܪܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܐܕܡ ܕܡܢ ܥܦܪܐ ܐܬܢܣܒ. ܘܒܝܕ ܚܛܝܬܗ ܐܬܓܙܪܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܠܐܝܟܐ ܢܗܦܘܟ. ܗܐ ܡܢܗ ܐܝܬܝ ܐܢ ܬܐܠܦ ܨܒܝܬ. ܕܘܟܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܚܪܬܐ ܗܪܟܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܝ ܨܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܠܝ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܒܚܫܐ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܽܘ ܟܕ ܐܬܬܒܥ ܐܡܪ ܘܦܠܝ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܬܬܩܢܬ ܠܗ ܕܘܟܬܐ. ܐܝܟ ܥܝܕܐ ܕܐܟ̈ܣܢܝܐ. ܘܕܡܟ ܐܝܟ ܟܠܢܫ. ܐܢܫܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܕܥܝܪܐ ܗܝ ܫܢܬܗܘܢ. ܘܡܬܒܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܐܚܘܬܐ. ܐܝܕܐ ܕܦܪܘܦܐܝܬܐ ܒܬܪ ܕܡܬܬܢܝܚܐ ܩܝܡܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܡܬܓܗܢܝܢ. ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܒܬܪܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܬܘܒ ܢܦܩܝܢ ܠܒܪ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܓܘ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܐܣ̈ܛܘܐ ܒܝܪܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܡܨܠܝܢ. ܘܚܙܘ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܐܘܬܐ ܕܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܡܟ ܡܚܕܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܗܘܐ ܒܦܠܓܗ̇ ܕܫܢܬܗ. ܘܬܠܐ ܪܝܫܗ ܘܚܙܐ ܕܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܡܟܢܫܝܢ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ: ܘܠܐ ܕܡܝܟܘ ܟܠܗܘܢ. ܘܐܓܪ ܩܠܝܠ. ܘܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܕܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܐܬܟܢܫ: ܕܠܐ ܢܚܘܐ ܢܦܫܗ ܕܩܐܡ. ܐܬܟܪܟ ܒܓܠܝܡܗ ܘܢܦܠ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܬܦܘܝܬܗ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܪܟܝܟܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܫܡܥ ܗܘܐ ܟܕ ܒܟܐ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܫܡܥ ܗܘܐ ܩܠ ܢܩܘܫܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ. ܟܕ
said: "Why? What need is there to inquire? When you see that | ama son of Adam, know that | am dust like Adam who was taken from dust, and upon whom through his sin sentence was given whither he should return. Lo! | come from him if you wish to learn; for | have no other place here; but pray that | may have one". These things that blessed man said with emotion when he was questioned, and ceased speaking. And so a place was prepared for him according to the custom in the case of strangers, and he fell asleep like everyone else. But there were some whose sleep was wakeful, and who used to watch the brotherhood, who after the house was at rest would stand in different places, while some bowed themselves down, and some knelt, and some again went outside, and so knelt in corners (ywvia) and in corridors (στοά), and prayed; and they saw that this blessed man from the weariness caused by his journey fell asleep at once; and again it happened that in the middle of his sleep he raised his head and saw that the servitors were not yet gathered in and all had not fallen asleep, and he waited a short time; and, when he saw that everyone was gathered in, that he might not show himself to be up, he wrapped himself in his coat, and fell upon his face on his rug; and this while he was weeping gently that no one might hear, until he heard the sound of the first bell, while the blessed men


[p. 565] {#po.18.p565}
ܣܝܡܝܢ ܒܗ ܛܘ̈ܒܢܐ ܒܠܐܐ ܟܠܗ ܠܝܠܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܫܡܥ ܩܠ ܢܩܘܫܐ. ܦܫܛ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܬܦܘܝܬܗ. ܘܐܝܟܡܢ ܗܘ ܕܗܐ ܝܩܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܛܒܥ ܒܫܢܬܗ ܩܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܘܟܕ ܩܡܘ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܪܝ ܘܫܡܫܘ. ܬܘܒ ܗܝ ܟܕ ܗܝ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܨܦܪܐ ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܟܐ ܗܘܐ ܟܕ ܫܕܐ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ. ܒܝܕ ܗܘܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܩܘܡ ܘܢܚܘܐ ܠܐܢܫ ܥܡܠܗ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܐܬܪ̈ܒܠܝ ܘܐܬܝ ܠܠܘܒܚܢܐ ܠܦܝܢܐ. ܒܨܦܪܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܪܝܫ ܕܝܪܐ ܘܫܪܟܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܪܝ ܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ. ܐܡܪܝܢ ܠܗ. ܕܡܢܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܥܝܢ̈ܝܟ ܐܒܘܢ. ܗܽܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܪ ܠܗܘܢ. ܕܐܐܪ ܕܐܬܡܠܝ ܦܚܩܢܝ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܐܬܪ̈ܒܠܝ ܥܝ̈ܢܝ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܚܙܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܦܠܚ ܠܝܠܝܐ ܟܠܗ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܫܒܘܩܘܢ ܠܗ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ. ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܢܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܬܛܝܪܐ ܕܕܝܪܐ. ܘܐܫܟܚ ܕܘܟܐ ܚܕܐ ܕܡܓܢܝܚܐ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܬܓܗܢ ܬܡܢ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܢܩܘܫܐ ܕܦܠܓܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ. ܟܕ ܒܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܟܠܗ̇ ܕܝܪܐ. ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܡܬܟܪܟܝܢ ܐܫܟܚܘܗܝ ܕܓܗܝܢ ܘܒܟܐ. ܐܡܪܝܢ ܠܗ. ܦܩܘܕ ܠܓܘ ܐܒܐ. ܕܒܥܐ ܥܠܝܟ ܪܝܫ ܐܚܐ. ܗܽܘ ܙܝܢܟ ܐܬܐ ܘܐܡܪ ܠܗ ܪܝܫ ܕܝܪܐ. ܐܝܟܐ ܗܼܘܝܬ ܐܒܐ. ܐܡܪ
were marking him the whole night. And, when he heard the sound of the bell, he stretched himself on his rug, and was rising with everyone else just as if he had been heavily sunk in his sleep. And, when the houseworkers had risen and recited the service the same again went on till morning, and thus he would weep lying on his face, in order that he might not rise and show his labour to anyone, until his eyes became swollen and greatly inflamed. And in the morning, when the archimandrite and the [houseworkers also saw his eyes, they say to him: "What has happened to your eyes, our father?". But he on his side said to them: "The air (ἀήρ) of yesterday affected me on the road, and therefore [ my eyes swelled But those who had seen the manner in which he had been employed all the night continued saying "Leave the blessed man alone". During the day he went out into the precinct of the monastery, and found a concealed spot, and there he bowed himself till the midday bell while they were seeking him all over the monastery; and then as they were going round they found him bowed down and weeping. They say to him: "Be so good as to go in, father; for the archimandrite is asking for you". He came; and the archimandrite said to him: "Where have you been, father?". He says to him:


[p. 566] {#po.18.p566}
ܠܗ. ܫܒܘܩ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ. ܒܓܢܬܐ ܡܬܟܪܡ ܗܘܝܬ. ܘܚܙܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܝ̈ܠܢܐ. ܐܡܪ ܠܗ ܪܝܫ ܕܝܪܐ. ܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܝܕܥ ܐܢܐ ܕܬܚܙܐ ܐܝ̈ܠܢܐ ܕܓܢܬܐ ܝܨܝܦ ܐܢܬ ܐܒܐ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܒܩܕ ܐܝܟ ܡܢ ܕܡܦܫܚܝ ܡܢ ܥܘܡܠܐ. ܕܢܬܬܢܝܚܝ ܩܕܡ ܥܕܢ ܕܪܡܫܐ. ܒܪܚܡܬ ܚܘܒܐ ܐܝܡ ܥܚܕܐ. ܗܽܘ ܕܝܢ ܢܦܠ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܡܦܝܣ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܡܪ. ܡܛܘܠ ܕܣܓܝ ܫܚܝܩ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܬܡܠܝ: ܘܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܐܟܠܬ ܒܪܡܠܦܐ. ܘܐܪܓܫܬ ܒܐܣܛܘܡܟܝ. ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܒܘܩ ܠܝ ܕܐܓܪ ܥܡ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܪܝ. ܕܬܬܦܫܪ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܬܬܢܝܚ ܥܡܗܘܢ. ܘܟܕ ܣܓܝ ܐܠܨܗ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܛܦܝܣ. ܫܒܩܗ ܕܢܣܥܘܪ ܐܝܟܡܢ ܕܨܒܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܟܕ ܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܫܠܡܬ. ܠܓܘܢܝܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ ܐܙܠ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܟܕ ܦܪ̈ܝܣܢ ܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܒܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ. ܓܥܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܒܟܬܐ ܘܐܡܪ. ܕܡܢܘ ܡܪܝ ܕܣܦܩ ܕܚܠܦܝ ܚܛܝܐ ܢܦܝܣܟ. ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܫܦܝܥܝܢ ܥܠ ܓܢܣܢ. ܛܠܘܡܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܪܝ ܘܡܪܓܙܢܐ. ܒܥܘܬܐ ܠܥܘܕܪܢܝ. ܒܥܘ ܡܢܟ ܡܪܝ ܠܐ ܬܫܒܩܝܢܝ. ܦܪܘܩܝܢܝ ܡܪܝ ܘܐܬܬܢܝܚ. ܥܕ ܠܐ ܐܙܠ ܐܢܐ ܘܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܦܪܘܩ ܚܝܝ ܡܢ ܚܒܠܐ ܕܚ̈ܛܗܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܝ. ܨܒܝ
"Forgive me, sir. | was going round the garden and seeing the trees". The archimandrite says to him: "| also know that you desire to see the trees of the garden, father" Then with the usual kindness he bade him, inasmuch as he was worn out with labour, go to rest before the vesper hour. But he fell on his face and continued entreating and saying: "Because | was greatly worn out yesterday, | both ate inordinately last night and caused a pain in my stomach (στόμαχος); but for God's sake allow me to wait with the houseworkers, in order that my previous food may digest itself, and so go to rest with them". And, when he had pressed him strongly and he would not give way, he allowed him to do as he wished And the blessed man, when the service was over, went to the corner (γωνία) where he had been before; and so with his hands stretched out to heaven in the form (TUTTo0¢) of a cross he continued crying with tears and saying: "Who is there, my Lord, who is sufficient to entreat thee for me the sinner, except thy great mercy that is poured out upon our race (yévoc)? | am ungrateful, my Lord, and a provoker. Come, | pray, to my aid. Abandon me not, | pray thee, my Lord. Save me, my Lord, and let me be at peace, before | go and am no more. Save my life from the corruption of my sins, Lord my God. 'Be pleased, Lord, to deliver me’


[p. 567] {#po.18.p567}
ܡܪܝܐ ܠܡܦܨܝܘܬܢܝ! ܡܢ ܕܘܕ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܕܓܠܘܬܐ ܘܡܬܛܥܝܢܘܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܘܡܢ ܟܠ ܕܒܗ. ܘܪܚܡ ܥܠܝ ܒܝܘܡ ܕܝܢܟ.. ܥܡ ܫܪܟܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܡܫܡܫܢܝܟ. ܡܛܠ ܫܡܟ ܩܕܝܫܐ: ܬܘܟܠܢܝ ܕܡܢ ܛܠܝܘܬܝ. ܘܟܕ ܙܥܩ ܗܘܐ ܚܫܠܬܢܐܝܬ ܒܕܡܥܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܓܥܐ ܗܘܐ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܽܘ: ܘܣܒܪ ܗܘܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܩܪܝܒܝܢ ܐܘ ܙܒܝܢ ܠܗܼ ܐܘ ܫܡܥܝܢ.. ܩܝܡܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܢ ܒܣܬܪ ܐܣܬܐ.. ܘܫܡܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܩܠ ܚ̈ܢܓܬܗ. ܘܬܗܪܝܢ ܒܚܫܗ ܘܒܕܡ̈ܥܘܗܝ ܕܩܕܡܟ ܐܠܗܐ. ܒܬܪ ܕܝܢ ܬܠܬܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ: ܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܣܘܒܪ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܘܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܥܘܡܪܐ: ܕܐܦ ܕܒܫܬ ܡܪܝ ܘܗܢܘܢ ܚܦܝܨܝܢ ܠܡܪܗܛ ܒܟܠܗ ܚܝܠܗܘܢ ܒܬܪ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܝܬܪܘܬܐ܂. ܫܕܪ ܠܗ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܣܬܘܐ ܒܗ ܒܥܘܡܪܐ. ܘܠܦܫܪܗ̇ ܕܫܢܬܐ.. ܠܗܘܢ ܠܛܘܪܐ ܢܨܦܢܐ ܗܘܐ. ܩܪܒ ܕܝܢ ܠܘܬ ܪܝܫܕܝܪܐ ܘܐܡܪ ܠܗ. ܕܐ̱ܢܐ ܡܢ ܡܪܝ ܂. ܡܛܠ ܡܚܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܝ ܘܕܚܠܬܐ ܕܣܬܘܐ ܘܕܩܘܪܫܐ܂. ܠܬܝܡܢܐ ܡܬܛܝܒ ܗܘܝܬ ܕܐ̱ܚܝܬ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܐܛܡ ܡܪܢ ܥܕܪܢܝ ܕܠܘܬܟܘܢ ܐܗܘܐ ܒܗܢܐ ܣܬܘܐ.. ܐܸܢ ܡܢܚ ܠܟܘܢ ܡܫܬܪܐ ܐܢܐ ܣܬܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܘܬܟܘܢ. ܘܐܢ ܗܘ ܕܚܝ ܐܢܐ ܘܨܒܐ ܡܪܢ. ܠܢܝܣܢ ܦܢܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܐܦܝ ܓܪܒܝܐ. ܪܝܫ
from the confusions and falseness and the deceitfulness of this world, and from all that is in it; and have mercy upon me in the day of thy judgment, with all the rest of thy servants, for the sake of thy holy Name, my trust from my boyhood". While that blessed man was thus mightily crying with many tears, and thought that there were no persons near or seeing or hearing him, some men were standing behind the wall, and listening to the sound of his sighs, and marvelling at his sorrow and tears before God. After three days, when he had seen the doings and practices of the convent, that even the very houseworkers also were eager to run with all their might after practices of excellence, he made up his mind to pass the winter in the same convent, and at the coming of the milder season to return to the same mountains. And he came to the archimandrite and said to him: "For my part, sir, | intended on account of my bodily weakness and fear of the winter and the cold to go down to the south; and, since perhaps our Lord sent me to be with you this winter, if it is convenient to you | will stay with you this winter; and, if so be | am alive and our Lord is willing, in Nisan | will


[p. 568] {#po.18.p568}
ܕܝܪܐ ܕܝܢ ܘܐܦ ܫܪܟܐ ܕܟܠܗ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܚܕܝܘ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܐ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ.. ܕܥܕܡܐ ܠܫܢܬܐ ܘܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܘܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ: ܐܸܢ ܨܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܠܡܗܘܐ.. ܚܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܗ ܟܕ ܡܫܩܪܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܪܝܫ ܕܝܪܐ. ܘܩܒܠܝܘܗܝ ܘܐܡܪܝܢ ܠܗ ܡܦܝܣܝܢܢ ܐܒܝܘܢ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܠܘܬܢ ܘܬܒܢܐ ܠܢ ܒܚܙܬܟ.. ܘܒܡܠܬܟ ܡܘܬܪܢܝܬܐ. ܘܡܢ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܣܡ ܒܪܥܝܢܗ ܕܢܟܬܪ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܥܦܝܦܐܝܬ ܥܡܠ ܗܘܐ. ܟܕ ܫܬܝܩ ܘܡܚܦܝ ܪܝܫܗ ܘܒܟܐ. ܐܦ ܟܕ ܢܛܥܡ ܘܒܠ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܝܬܒ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܕܡ̈ܥܘܗܝ ܡܬܟܠܝܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܕܢܬܟܠܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܛܥܡ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܪܟܢ ܪܝܫܗ ܠܬܚܬܐ ܘܒܟܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܠܝܓ ܠܝܓ ܡܠܬܐ ܡܢ ܦܘܡܗ ܠܐ ܢܦܩܐ ܗܘܬ܂. ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܘܬܘܕܝܬܐ܆. ܘܬܐܢ̈ܚܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܩܛܥܢ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܘܐ ܙܒܢܐ.. ܐܙܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܒܛܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܥܠ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ܂. ܘܕܝܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܛܪܕܝܢ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܝܗܘܢ.. ܘܡܦܝܣܝܢ ܠܗ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܠܗܘܢ ܢܝܢܐ ܕܕܘܒܪܐ. ܘܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܕܠܘܩܒܠ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܝܗܘܢ ܕܦܐܕܐ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ. ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܒܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܦܠ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܬܟܫܦ ܗܘܐ ܟܕ ܐܡܪ ܕܫܒܘܩܘܢ
return toward the north". And the archimandrite and all the other members of the convent also rejoiced, since this was indeed their custom (νόμος), that, if a stranger wished to stay as long as a year and two years and ten, they rejoiced over him, honouring him in every respect like the archimandrite. And they consented and they say to him: "We beg you, our father, to be [with us and edify us by the sight of you, and by your helpful conversation", And from that time he determined to remain; and so he thenceforth doubled his labour, keeping silence and hiding his head and weeping. Even when eating and sitting at table, even so also his tears could not be restrained, but he would eat and bow his head down and weep; and so no words came rapidly out of his mouth except prayer and thanksgiving, and unceasing sighs. When some time had passed, those who cared about methods of life, and judged and repelled their thoughts, used to go and beg him one by one to tell them a rule of life and an illustration of the method of contending against the thoughts of the rebellious fiends. But he in great humility would fall upon his face and make entreaty, saying: "Forgive me,


[p. 569] {#po.18.p569}
ܠܝ ܐܚ̈ܝ. ܕܟܪܝܗ ܐܢܐ ܂. ܘܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ. ܘܠܐ ܬܒܥܘܢ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܚܛܝܐ ܨܒܘ̈ܬܐ ܕܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ. ܐܬܛܦܝܣܘܢ ܡܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܕܐܡܪ. ܕܐܢ ܒܕܫܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ ܠܐ ܐܫܟܚ ܐܢܫ ܕܢܕܒܪ ܫܦܝܪ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܥܕܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܣܦܩ ܠܡܕܒܪܘ. ܘܗܫܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܚܫܘܟܐ ܕܚܛܝܝܬܐ ܝܬܒ ܐܢܐ ܐܠܐ ܚܘܘ ܚܘܒܟܘܢ ܘܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ ܘܟܕ ܙܒܢܝܢ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܐܠܨ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܪܝܫܕܝܪܐ ܕܢܡܠܠ.. ܐܡܪ ܠܗ ܗܘ ܒܚܪܬܐ. ܐܒܐ ܚܝ̈ܘܐ ܚܘܒܟ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܨܠܐ ܥܠܝ. ܥܡ ܫܪܟܐ ܕܥܘܡܪܟ ܘܠܪܗ. ܐܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܝܘܡܐ ܡܛܐ ܠܗ ܕܐܚܙܘܩ.. ܘܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܐܢܐ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܪܢܝܢ ܕܠܛܘܪܐ ܐܡܪ ܕܐܚܙܘܩ.. ܐܡܪܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܬܬܢܝܚ ܐܒܘ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܓܝܪ ܣܝܬܘܐ ܗܘ ܘܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܡܫܟܚ ܕܢܙܚܘܩ ܝܘܡܢܐ. ܐܡܪ ܠܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܒܟܐ. ܘܐܦ ܠܐ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܐܬܐ ܣܬܘܐ. ܐܠܐ ܗܐ ܐܬܐ. ܕܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܡܫܟܚ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܡܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܘܠܐ ܠܡܚܙܩ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܠܡܦܠܚ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܐܒܗܘ̈ܝ ܘܐܚܝ ܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ. ܗܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܘܡܪܝ. ܥܕܪܢܝ ܓܝܪ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܘܬܟܘܢ.. ܕܒܝܕ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܒܥܘܬܟܘܢ. ܐܬܚܢܢ ܒܝܘܡܐ ܗ̇ܘ. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܕܪܟܘܢܝ ܚ̈ܛܗܝ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܟܚܬ ܠܡܚܙܐ. ܐܚܝ ܐܠܘ ܚܘܠܦܝ.. ܕܐܬܦܨܐ ܡܢ ܬܫܢܝܩܐ. ܩܨܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܕܪܟܢܝ. ܘܠܒܬܪ
brethren, for | am sick, and pray for me; and, seek not such things at the hands of me a sinner. Be persuaded by the blessed apostle who says: ‘If a man hath not been able to rule his own house aright, how is he capable of ruling God's church?’ And now | for my part am sitting in the darkness of sin; but show your love and pray for me". And, when the archimandrite often pressed him to speak, he himself said to him at last: "Father, show your love to God and pray for me, with all the rest of your convent. For as for me the day has come for me to set out on my journey, and | am not sufficient for it". They, thinking that he meant "| am setting out for the mountains", say to him: "Rest yourself, our father. It is still winter and no man can set out on a journey to-day". He says to them weeping: "Nay indeed, the winter has not yet come, but it is just coming; in which as you say no man can either set out on a journey nor yet work And now, my fathers and my brothers, pray for me. This is my convent; for God sent me to you, that through the remembrance of your petition | may find mercy in that day. My sins have overtaken me and | could not see it. My brethren, pray on my behalf, that | may escape from the torment. The end has overtaken me, and the day after to-morrow | shall depart from this life to


[p. 570] {#po.18.p570}
ܡܚܪ ܡܢ ܚ̈ܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܿܦܢܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܪܢ. ܡܟܝܠ ܡܦܝܣ ܐܢܐ ܠܟܠ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܟܘܢ! ܕܢܦܝܣ ܚܘܠܦܝ.. ܘܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܚ̈ܝ ܐܬܚܝܠܝܘ. ܫܦܝܪ ܪܗܛܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܦܠܘܚܘ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܚܝܘܬܟܘܢ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܫܘܠܡܐ. ܒܟܠ ܓܝܪ ܕܝܨܦܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ.. ܚܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܬܒܥܝܐ. ܟܠ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܒܟܠ ܡܫܡܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܘܡܫܟܿܚܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܦ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܡܪܬܝܘ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗܢܐ ܐܒ̈ܗܝ ܘܐܚ̈ܝ. ܐܝܟ ܡܿܢ ܕܐܦ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܝܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܚܘܒܟܘܢ ܡܢܝ ܬܒ݀ܥ ܗܘܐ ܕܐܡܿܠܠ.. ܗܟܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܐܗܘܐ ܛܠܝܡܐ ܕܚܘܒܟܘܢ ܘܕܨܒܝܢܟܘܢ ܛܒܐ.. ܗܐ ܡܿܥܗܕ ܐܢܐ ܠܟܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܟܘܢ.. ܟܕ ܠܘ ܡܠܦܘ ܡܿܠܦ ܐܢܐ ܠܟܘܢ.. ܐܠܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܗܝܢ ܗܳܪܓ݂ܺܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ. ܡܪܢ ܩܪܝܒ ܗܘ. ܕܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܬܐܨܦܝܘܢ. ܐܠܐ ܒܟܠ ܙܒܢ ܕ̈ܠܘܬܟܘܢ ܘܫܐܠܬܟܘܢ ܒܬܬܝܕܥܢ! ܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ. ܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܡ ܕܚܙܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ.. ܕܥܘ ܕܡܛܬ ܠܗ ܠܬܪܥܐ. ܙܕܩ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܗܟܐ ܠܡܕܥ ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ.. ܕܡܛܬ ܠܬܪܥܐ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܬܕܓܠܐ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܗܘ ܕܐܡܼܪ. ܘܬܘܒ ܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܝܕܥܝܢܢ
our Lord. Accordingly | entreat you one and all to make entreaty on my behalf; and you yourselves also, my brethren, be strong. You are running well Perform this your life-service unto the end; for in every matter about which you concern yourselves there is one object that is sought. You know all; you are perfect in all; and you are able to admonish others also. And therefore, my fathers and brothers, inasmuch as a few days ago also your affection required of me to speak these words, now in order not to be ungrateful for your affection and your good will, lo! | put you in mind of these your principles, not teaching you, but things on which you are meditating at all times. ‘Our Lord is at hand. Be anxious for nothing, but always let your prayers and requests be made known before God! For, 'when ye see all these things, know that it has reached the door’ For from this time we must truly know that it has reached the door; for the word of him who said it is not falsified. And again we know that this also is written, ‘There is great fear of falling into the hands of the living God! and again 'We shall all stand before that dread judgment-seat (βῆμα)
ܕܟܬܝܼܒܐ. ܕܕܚܠܬܐ ܗܝ ܪܒܬܐ ܠܡܦܠ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܚܝܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܕܟܠܢ ܥܬܝܕܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܠܡܩܡ ܩܕܡ ܒܝܡܐ ܕܚܝܠܬܐ ܕܟܐܢܘܬܐ. ܕܟܠ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܢ ܢܬܦܪܥ ܡܕܡ ܕܥܒܝܕ ܠܗ. ܐܢ ܕܛܒ ܘܐ ܕܒܝܫ. ܢܪܢܐ ܐܚ̈ܝ ܒܗܠܝܢ. ܢܕܚܠ ܚܒܝ̈ܒܝ ܡܢ ܗܳܝ ܦܥܬܐ ܕܚܝܠܬܐ ܕܦܣ̈ܝܩܬܐ. ܢܙܘܥ ܡܢ ܗܽܘ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܠܒܝܟ ܟܠ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܢ ܚܘܫܒܢܐ ܒܨܒܥܬܗ. ܘܬܠܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܘܓܝܠܐ ܨܘܪܗ ܘܥܒܝܕ ܗܘ ܠܗ ܡܩܛܪܓܢܐ ܩܕܡ ܟܐܢܘܬܐ. ܡܢ ܗܝ ܫܥܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܢܘ ܐܘ ܡܬܦܠܛ. ܡܢܘ ܡܬܚܟܡ. ܕܒܡܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܡܢܘ ܕܢܬܛܫܐ. ܡܢܘ ܢܐܡܪ ܕܙܟܝ ܐܢܬ ܠܒܝ ܘܐܬܕܟܝܬ ܡܢ ܚܛܗ̈ܐ. ܡܟܝܠ ܐܚ̈ܝ ܐܝܟ ܡܿܢ ܕܒܗܠܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܟܠܢ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܦܝܣܝܢܢ.. ܗܘ̈ܝ ܨܝܪ̈ܢ ܩܕܡ ܥܝ̈ܢܝܢ ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ. ܘܟܬܝܒܢ ܥܠ ܠܘ̈ܚܝܐ ܕܠܒܝܢ. ܕܗܟܢܐ ܟܕ ܡܿܬܬܢܝܚܝܢܢ ܩܕܡ ܗܿܝ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܠܝܬ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ. ܟܠ ܝܘܡ̈ܝ ܚܝ̈ܝܢ. ܐܦ ܐܢ ܡܕܡ ܐܚܪܝܢ ܠܐ ܣܝ̈ܦܩܝܢ ܚܢܢ. ܒܙܘܥܐ ܘܥܕܢܐ ܪܘܢܝܐ ܘܕܚܝܠܐ ܬܦܪܘܣ ܟܢ̈ܦܝܗ̇ ܘܬܚܦܐ ܠܢ. ܘܢܫܬܘܙܒ ܒܗ ܡܢ ܗܽܘ ܬܫܢܝܩܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܠܐ ܙܕܩ ܠܢ ܕܦܫܝܩܐܝܬ ܘܐܝܟ ܫܘܥܝܬܐ ܗܼܘܝܢ ܫܡܿܥܝܢ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܐܠܐ ܒܕܚܠܬܐ
of righteousness, that each one of us may be requited for that which has been wrought by him, be it good or be it evil’ Let us think upon these things my brethren. Let us, my beloved, fear the dread final hour. Let us dread the moment when each one of us shall receive an account upon his fingers, and his hands shall be hung up and his neck shall be laid bare and he shall be made an accuser against himself in the presence of justice. For who shall contrive a means of shunning or escaping that hour, since who is there who shall be hidden by his eloquence? 'Who shall say "Thou art innocent, my heart, and thou hast been purified from sins"?' Accordingly, my brethren, inasmuch as we are all abundantly persuaded of these things, let them be depicted before our eyes at all times, and written on the tablets of our heart that so, while we rest before the grace that is full of mercy all the days of our life, even if we are not capable of doing aught else, at that moment of trepidation and fear it may spread its wings and cover us, and we may by it be delivered from the eternal torment, of which we ought not to hear with indifference as if it were a tale, but with great fear and mighty trembling". While then that blessed man was saying all these things in the presence


[p. 571] {#po.18.p571}
ܪܒܬܐ ܘܒܙܘܥܬܐ ܥܡܝܩܬܐ. ܟܕ ܗܟܝܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܽܘ ܒܚ̈ܢܓܬܗ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ: ܘܒܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܦܝܫܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܢ ܟܠܢ ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ: ܒܚܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܒܬܢܚܬܐ ܣܓܝܐܬܐ. ܒܟ ܐܦ ܠܢ ܕܢܫܡܥܝܘܗܝ ܟܕ ܛܒ ܬܗܝܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܒܗ ܕܚܙܝܢܢ ܕܡܕܡ ܕܟܪܝܗܘܬ ܦܓܪܐ ܒܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ. ܘܦܕܣܩ ܬܟܝܠܐܝܬ ܕܒܬܪ ܬܠܬܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ ܢ̇ܦܩ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܟܢܐ ܟܢܝܫܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܝܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܠܝܠܘ̈ܬܐ. ܗܢܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܢܝܢ ܚܬܬ ܘܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܟܠܢ ܒܠܝܠܝܐ ܕܝܢ ܗܽܘ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܬܘܒ ܐܦܝܣ.. ܕܬܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܨܠܘܬܐ.. ܘܢܬܬܣܝܡ ܦܝܪܡܐ. ܘܢܬܚܝܣܐ ܠܗ ܟܕ ܒܟܐ ܘܐܡܿܪ. ܕܟܬܝܒ ܕܠܐ ܙܟܐ ܩܕܡܝܟ ܟܠ ܕܚܝ. ܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܙܕܩ ܠܢ ܕܢܬܬܟܠ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܢ. ܘܟܕ ܟܠܢ ܒܪܟܢ: ܘܨܠܘܬܐ ܗܘܼܬ: ܘܟܕ ܡܕܒܚܐ ܐܬܦܬܚ. ܘܦܝܪܡܐ ܐܬܬܣܝܡ: ܘܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܒܚܝܦܐ ܐܚܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܶܬܡܠܠܢ.. ܩܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܘܒܟܐ. ܘܒܚܪܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܟܠܢ ܩܥܐ ܗܘܐ. ܐܚ̈ܝ ܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܬܫܒ̈ܚܬܝ ܬܢܐ ܐܢܐ ܩܕܡܝܟܘܢ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܗܽܘ ܕܡܿܫܒܚܝ ܢܦܫܗ ܗܽܘ ܗܘ ܒܝܩܝܪܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܢܫܒܚܝܘܗܝ. ܠܝ ܩܨܐ ܕܥܘܢܕܢܐ ܐܕܪܟܢܝ. ܘܠܘ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܐܢܫܝܐ ܝܫ ܡܬܒܥܐ. ܘܐܦ ܠܐ ܐܝܩܪܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܘܬܟܘܢ ܦܕܪ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܚܛܝܬܘܬܝ ܕܬܥܦܘܢ ܦܓܪܝ.. ܠܐ ܟܠܐ ܐܢܐ
of us all amidst his many sighs, and amidst copious tears, with great emotion and with many sobs, he caused us also to listen to him in great astonishment at him, since we saw that no sign of bodily infirmity was to be seen on him, and he confidently pronounced that after three days he would depart from the body. While we were thus gathered together, for two days and nights he sat and said the same things to us all; but on the third night he again requested that prayer might be made for him and incense set, and he might receive absolution while he wept and said: "It is written 'None that liveth shall be innocent before thee’ And, when we had all knelt, and prayer had been made, and when the altar had been opened, and incense set, and everyone was overpowered with sorrow on account of the words spoken, he was standing and weeping, and at last he continued crying to all of us: "My brethren, pray for me; for | am not repeating my praises before you; 'he who praiseth himself is not he who is approved, but he whom God praiseth' Upon me has come the end of death; and human praise is no longer needed by me, nor yet honour; but, since God sent my sinfulness to you to bury my body, | will not prevent Therefore we must not trust in ourselves


[p. 572] {#po.18.p572}


[p. 573] {#po.18.p573}
ܡܢܟܘܢ ܕܬܕܥܘܢ܇. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܡܥܕܪܢܘܬܗ ܣܥܕܪ ܥܡܝ ܒܟܠܗܘܢ ܝܘ̈ܡܝ. ܝܘܡܢܐ ܗܐ ܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܬܫܥ ܫܢܝ̈ܐ ܠܒܫܬ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ. ܒܛܘܼܪܵܐ ܘܒܡܥܪ̈ܐ ܘܒܝܬ ܒܢ̈ܝܢܫܐ. ܘܡܢ ܟܕ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ ܫܩܠܬ. ܠܐ ܐܝܬ ܥܡ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܕܡ. ܠܐ ܦܩܬ ܕܓܿܕ̣ܠܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܣܵܦܘܬܝ. ܠܐ ܡܠܠܬ ܠܒ̈ܢܝܢܫܐ ܒܝ̣ܚܦܐܝܬ ܐܘ ܨܚܝܬ ܠܐܢܫ. ܠܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܡܠܬܝ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܡܪܢ. ܐܝܢ ܐܝܢ. ܘܠܐ ܠܐܼ. ܠܐ ܐܡܪܬ ܐܝܢ ܕܠܘ ܒܿܫܪܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܠܐ. ܕܠܐ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܫܪܝܪ. ܠܐ ܒܖܥܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܝ ܡܘܬܪ ܘܠܐܚܪܝܢ ܡܿܚܣܪܿ. ܠܐ ܪܛܢܬ ܥܠ ܐܠܗܝ ܒܛܘܪ̈ܦܝ ܘܒܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܝ ܘܗܼܘ ܣܗܕ ܠܝ. ܗܢܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܒܛܘܪ̈ܦܐ ܕܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܠܐ ܪܛܢܬ݀ ܒܠܒܝ ܥܠ ܡܢ ܕܠܐ ܩܒܠܢܝ ܘܐܢܝܚܢܝ. ܠܐ ܐܬܒܨܪ ܠܝ ܗܽܘ ܕܨܚܝܢܝ ܘܛܪܕܢܝ ܡܢ ܗܽܘ ܕܝܩܪܢܝ ܘܐܢܝܚܢܝ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܝ ܩܕܡܝܟܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܠܐܠܗܐ ܓܠܝ̈ܝܢ ܬܢܝܕܬ. ܡܛܘܠ ܕܚܙܝܬ ܕܐܦ ܠܟܘܢ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܢܚ̈ܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܣܝܡܼܝܢ. ܘܠܘ ܡܛܠ ܕܒܝ ܬܬܕܡܘܢ. ܐܠܐ ܟܠܢ ܒܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܠܗܢ ܗܽܘ ܡܠܦܢܗܝܢ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܢܬ݀ܕܡܐ܇ ° ܡܛܠ ܕܗܼܘ ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ
any of you from knowing the things that he worked with me by his help all my days. It is to-day thirty-nine years that | have worn this habit (σχῆμα), on mountains and in caves and among men; and, since | received the habit (σχῆμα), | have not possessed aught beside Christ. No lie has come out of my lips; | have not spoken evil words to men or reviled any man. My speech has been naught but as our Lord said, 'Yea, yea’ and 'Nay, nay' | have not said ‘Yea’ unless it was in truth 'Yea’, nor yet 'Nay' without it also being true. | have not sought anything that would benefit me and harm another. | have not murmured against my God during my troubles and distresses and he himself is my witness. This is the time during which amid the troubles of pilgrimage (ξενία) | have not murmured in my heart against any man who did not receive me and entertain me. The man who reviled me and drove me away was not treated by me with less respect than the man who honoured and entertained me. And these things, my brethren, | have repeated before you even as they are manifest before God, because | saw that these objects lie before you too, and not in order that you may imitate me, but let us all imitate Christ our God the teacher of these things, because he himself truly taught this humility in his person, and showed it to us. My


[p. 574] {#po.18.p574}
ܡܘܟܟܐ ܗܢܐ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ ܐܠܦ ܘܚܘܝ ܠܢ. ܚ̈ܝ ܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ ܘܦܘܫܘ ܒܫܠܡܐ. ܐܠܗܐ ܢܢܛܪ ܟܢܫܟܘܢ. ܥܠ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܠܒܝܬ ܕܝܩ̈ܘܢܝܩܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܨܠܐ ܘܟܼܫܛ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܐܓܕܼܣ ܬܡܢ. ܟܕ ܟܠܢܫ ܡܿܣܟܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܦܘܩ. ܘܥܠ ܪܝܫ ܕܝܪܐ ܘܚܙܝܗܝ ܕܡܓܕܸܣ ܥܠ ܩܘܦܣܐ ܘܐܡܼܪ ܠܗ. ܐܼܒܐ ܦܩܘܕ ܥܠ ܬܫܘܝܬܐ ܐܓܣ. ܘܟܕ ܡܠܠ ܥܡܗ. ܢܦܫܗ ܐܫܠܡ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܘܟܕ ܗܕܐ ܗܼܘܬ܂. ܕܚܠܝܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܢܦܠܬ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܕܚܠܬܐ ܐܦܩܘܢܝܗܝ ܒܛܘܒܢܐ. ܘܐܬܩܒܪ ܒܙܘܚܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܡܗ̈ܝܡܢܐ ܕܡܕܝܢܬܐ. ܐܠܗܐ ܕܦܠܚ ܢܘܓܪܐ ܕܫܢܝ̈ܐ܂. ܢܘܪܒ ܕܘܟܪܢܗ ܥܡ ܕܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܕܥܒܕܘ ܨܒܝܢܗ. ܘܕܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܝܠܕܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܪܝܡ.. ܘܚܢܢ ܢܬܥܕܪ ܒܫܘܦܥ ܨܠܘܬܗܘܢ..
brethren, pray for me and abide in peace. God keep your community" And the blessed man went into the vestry in order to pray and stretched himself out and lay down there, while everyone was looking for him to come out. And the archimandrite went in and saw him lying on a paving-stone and said to him: "Father, be so good as to lie upon a rug". And, when he had spoken to him, he gave up his soul to God. And on this happening great fear fell upon everyone; and so in fear they brought the blessed man out; and he was buried with a great procession of all the believers of the city. May God whom he served for a long term of years magnify his memory with that of the saints who did his will, and that of the holy God-bearer Mary, and may we for our part be helped through their prayer, Amen and Amen!
ܐܡܝܢ ܘܐܡܝܢ܀ ܫܠܼܡܬ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܡܿܠܟܐ ܡܣܪܩܐ ܘܐܟܣܢܝܐ܀
The history of Malkha the mendicant and stranger is ended.


[p. 575] {#po.18.p575}
ܬܘܒ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ܆ ܕܓܒܼܪܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܫܡܗ ܗܘܐ ܐܠܝܐ ܘܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܕܪܐ ܡܕܝܢܬܐ]܀
[Next the thirtieth history, of a man of god whose name was Elijah, who was in the city of Dara]
° ܐܬܬܡܢܥܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܕܝܘܡ̈ܝܬܐ ܒܒܝܬܗܼ ܥܡ ܣܓ̈ܝܐܐ ܗܼܘܝܢ. ܟܕ ܬܗܝܪܝܢܢ ܒܚܪܝܦܘܬ ܛܝܢܢܗ ܕܓܒܪܐ܇ ܟܕ ܩܐܡ ܘܡܚܪܡ ܘܩܪܐ ܠܐܦܝ̈ܣܩܘܦܐ ܘܠܫܪܟܐ ܕܐܚܝ̈ܕܝ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ.. ܪ̈ܫܝܥܐ ܘܟܦܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܝܗܘ̈ܕܝܐ ܚܕ̈ܬܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܡܿܠܟܐ. ܥܠܘܗܝ ܢܟܼܬܒܘܢ.. ܘܠܐܟܣܘܪܝܐ܂ ܢܦܩ. ܘܦܠܚܘܬܗ ܘܕܪܬܗ ܘܟܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܫܒܩ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ. ܕܢܝ̣ܣܒܘܢ° ܪ̈ܫܝܥܐ. ܐܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܡܪܝ ܝܫܘܥ ܒܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܢܦܩ. ܘܫܠܡ ܚܡܫܥܣܪܐ ܫܢܝ̈ܢ ܒܪܕܘܦܝܐ ܡܠܝܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܒܨܪ ܪܚܡܬܐ ܐܦܣܢܝܐ ܘ̈ܡܣܟܢܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܫܠܡ. ܒ]ܢܨܚܢܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܝܬܝܪܘܬܐ. ܟܕ ܠܘ
we arrived, in whose house we were with many others for some days, being amazed at the keenness of the man's enthusiasm, as he stood and uttered anathemas and called the bishops as well as those who wielded the authority of the crown to their faces impious men, renegades, and new Jews, insomuch that they wrote to the king about him, and he was driven into exile (ἐξορία). And he left his employment and his mansion and all that he possessed, saying, "Let the impious men take them; for my part | seek my Lord Jesus only"; and so he departed. And he completed fifteen years in banishment, without diminishing the kindness to strangers and poor men; and thus he ended his life [in] great distinction which surpasses description, his distinction not being one only. And out of his practices | have briefly


[p. 576] {#po.18.p576}
ܚܕ ̇ܗܘ ܢܨܚܢܗ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܡܪܗܛܐܝܬ ܡܢ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܪܫܡܬܢ. ܟܕ ܕܚܝܠ ܐܢܐ ܡܢ ܩܘܛܪܓܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܨܪܬ܀
set down these, while fearing to be accused on account of those which | have omitted.
ܫܠܼܡܬ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܓܒܪܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܫܡܗ ܗܘܐ ܐܠܝܐ܆܆ ܘܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܕܪܐ ܡܕܝܢܬܐ܀
The history of a man of God whose name was Elijah, who was in the city of Dara, is finished.
ܬܘܒ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܚܕܐ܆ ܕܐ̈ܚܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܓܪ̈ܐ܇ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܥܡ ܬܐܓܘܪܬܐ ܥܠܡܢܝܬܐ ܘܐܦ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܡܬܬܓܪܝܢ ܗܘܘܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܫܡ̈ܗܝܗܘܢ܆ ܐܠܝܐ
Next the thirty-first history, of two brothers who were traders, who beside worldly trade engaged moreover in divine also, whose names are Elijah and Theodore.
ܘܬܐܘܕܘܪܐ܀ ܡܛܘܠ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܥܠ ܐܢܦܐ ܕܪܕܘ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܕܚܠܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܣܝܡܼܐ ܠܢ ܕܢܥܬܕ܆ ܘܒܢܝܫܐ ܕܬܫܥܝܬܢ ܥܠ ܢܕܚܢܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢܿ ܢܘܕܥ܂. ܡܛܠ ܗܢܐ ܠܐ ܟܡܼܢܢ, ܐܦ ܠܐ ܥܠ ܢܨܚܢܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ܇. ܕܒܐܣ̈ܟܝܡܝܗܘܢ ܡܿܢ ܒܝ̈ܢܝ ܥܠܡܐ ܘܬܓܪ̈ܐ ܚܫܝܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ.. ܒܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܠܐܟܐ ܐܡܝܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ܂܀ ܘܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܛܠܝܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܬܓܪܘ ܥܡ ܬܓܪ̈ܐ܂ ܘܢܦܩܘ ܥܡܗܘܢ. ܟܠ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܠܦܢܝܬܗ. ܗܽܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܣܝܐ܂ ܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܘܣܝܡܼܘ
Since therefore it is our purpose to tell the stories of men who walked in the path of religion, and in the course of our history to make the glories of their practices known, accordingly neither have we kept the glories of these excellent men hidden, who in their garb (σχῆμα) were reckoned laymen and traders, but were constantly occupied in the practices of angels. These are two brothers, and in the time of their boyhood they served for pay with traders, and travelled with them, each of them in his own direction, that is, to the regions of the Persians both of them. And they formed in their mind


[p. 577] {#po.18.p577}
ܒܪܥܝܢܗܘܢܼ ܢܦܩܐ ܕܕܚܠܬ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܐܬܚܫܒܘ ܕܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܡܛܝܒ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܠܗܐ.. ܢܗܘܐ ܠܚܫܳܚܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ.. ܘܕܦܪܟܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܚܙܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܣܢܝܩܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܡܟܝܠ, ܥܒܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܦܠܚܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܡܿܦܠܓܝܢ ܠܡܣ̈ܟܢܐ ܘܠܐܟܣܢܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܛܢܕܢܗܘܢ.. ܪܬܚ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ ܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܪܡܿܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܗ ܐܝ̈ܕܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܘܪܟܬܐ ܫܪܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܬܗܿܦܟ ܗܘܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܣܡܼܘ ܒܪܥܝܢܗܘܢ܂. ܕܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܿܥܒܕܝܢ ܬܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܒܝܫܐܝܬ ܠܡܼܣܥܪ.. ܕܢܬܪܚܩܘܢ܂ ܠܓܡܪ. ܗܽܢܘ ܕܝܢ܂ ܡܢ ܡܘܡ̈ܿܬܐ ܕܠܓܡܪ. ܘܡܢ ܕܓܠܘܬܐ܂ ܘܡܢ ܥܠܘܒܘܬܐ. ܘܡܢ ܡܬܩܠܐ ܘܡܬܩܠܐ ܘܡܢ ܟܝܠܐ ܘܟܝܠܐ. ܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܒܟܐܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܬܪܝܨܘܬܐ ܠܗܘ ܕܙܒܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܐܘ ܡܿܙܒܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘܼ ܕܠܐ ܚܪܝܢܐ. ܐܸܢ ܕܝܢ ܗܽܘ ܡܶܢ ܕܙܒܢ ܡܡܫܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܡܶܡܘܡܬܐ ܘܒܣܘܦܣܪܐ.. ܗܼܢܘܢ ܒܦܣ̈ܝ̈ܩܬܐ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗܼ ܗܟܢܐ. ܐܚܐ ܩܝܿܒܠ ܕܬܪܝܨ ܐܝܬ ܐܡܪܬ ܠܟ ܕܠܐ ܥܠܘܒܘܬܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܛܠܘܡܝܐ ܘܚܪܝܢܐ.. ܘܕܠܐ ܣܘܦܣܪܐ ܘܡܘ̈ܡܬܐ. ܐܢ ܠܒܕ ܠܟ ܙܒܘܢܼ ܐܘ ܙܒܝܢ. ܡܛܠ ܡܘܢ ܚܪܒܬ
the purpose of following a religious life; and they determined that whatever God provided for them should go to their own use, and that of everyone else whom they saw to be a stranger like them and in need. And thus they thenceforth did; for they carried on their business, and distributed to the poor and to the strangers. And, when God saw their zeal, he caused everything to which they put their hand to increase abundantly in their hands. And thus a blessing rested on everything that passed through their hands, they having resolved to abstain entirely from the evil practices which traders of the world are wont to follow, i. e. from oaths of all kinds, and from lying, and from extortion, and from diverse weights and diverse measures They told everything to the man who was buying from them or selling to them fairly and honestly without contention; but, if that man whoever he might be began oaths and bargaining, they on their side would say to him shortly as follows: "Brother, accept what | have told you honestly, without extortion and without fraud and contention, and without bargaining and oaths. If it suits you buy" (or "sell"). "Wherefore are you making havoc of (7) oaths and allowing Satan to thrust you into sin, when you are under no necessity
ܡܘ̈ܡܬܐ ܘܙܪ̈ܒ ܠܟ ܣܛܢܐ ܕܬܚܛܐ܇ ܟܕ ܩܛܝܪܐ ܠܐ ܣܝܡ ܥܠܝܟ ܕܐܸܢ ܒܥܝܬܿ ܐܘ ܠܐ ܒܥܝܬ ܬܙܒܢ, ܐܘ ܬܙܒܝܢ. ܗܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܘܡ̈ܬ ܸܢ ܠܒܕ ܠܟ ܣܒ ܘܐܸܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܨܠܐ ܥܠܝܢ܀ ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܢܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ ܒܕܚܠܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܥܒܕܗܘܢ ܦܠܚܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܀ ܟܕ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܘܬܠܬ ܫܢܝܢ.. ܒܚܡܫܐ ܚܡܫܐ ܐܘ ܫܬܐ ܫܬܐ ܕܝܢܪ̈ܐ ܐܓܪ ܗܘܐ ܘܟܕ ܚܙܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܦܠܚܝܢ ܥܡܗܘܢ.. ܕܟܠ ܕܥܿܒܕܝܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܿܨܠܚ ܗܘܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ܂. ܒܕܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܥܣܪܐ ܥܣܪܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܒܬܪ ܫܢܝ̈ܐ ܕܨܒܝܼܘ ܕܢܝܼܦܩܘܢ.. ܥܒܕܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܝܢܪ̈ܐ ܒܫܢܬܐ. ܒܚܪܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܚܙܘ ܒܘܪܟܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ ܡܬܦܪܢܣܐ ܗܘܬ ܬܠܬܝܢ ܬܠܬܝܢ݀ ܕܝܢܪ̈ܐ ܒܫܢܬܐ ܥܒܕܘ ܠܝܗܘܢ.. ܕܒܠܚܘܕ܂ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܢܟܬܪܘܢ. ܟܕ ܟܠ ܕܒܒ̈ܬܝܗܘܢ܂ ܐܓܥܠܘ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ. ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܦܬܝܩܬܐ ܪܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ܂ ܕܛܒ. ܘܒܐܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܣܓܺܝܐܐ ܡܬܗ̇ܦܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܗܼܢܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܚܝ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ: ܟܕ ܫܢܝ̈ܐ ܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܗܢܐ ܐܓܪܐ ܡܿܠܚܘ: ܘܙܢܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܥܡܠܗܘܢ ܕܟܐܢܘܬܐ܂. ܐܬܬܚܫܒܘ ܒܝܢܬܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪܝܢ.. ܕܦܩܠܚ ܗܘ ܠܢ ܕܚܢܢ ܢܐܙܠ ܢܦܠܘܚ ܒܫܘܠܛܢܢ ܢܦܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܗܢܐ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܦܪܢܣܬ ܠܢ.. ܕܗܘܼܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܠܢ ܘܦܫܝܩ܆.. ܕܡܢ ܗܽܘ ܡܝܕܡ ܕܝܗܒ ܠܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܢܬܠ ܠܝܗ ܕܠܐ ܩܢܛܐ ܕܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܠܐܬܪܗܘܢ ܗܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܐܡܕ
to buy" (or "sell") "whether you want or not? Now, if it suits you, take it without oaths; and if not, pray for us". And thus these excellent men followed their employment in the fear of God, each of them serving for two and three years at the salary of five or six denarii. And, when those under whom they were employed saw that God caused everything that they did to prosper in their hands, they appointed them ten denarii; and again, after some years during which they had been willing to travel, they appointed them twenty denarii a year; and at last, when they saw the blessing that grace brought about through them, they appointed them thirty denarii a year, that they might remain with them only, having committed to their hands all that was in their houses; and they had very large storehouses (ἀποθήκη), and they used to do business in many regions. But these excellent brothers, when they had completed twenty years at this salary, and Grace fed them out of their honest labour, deliberated with one another, saying, "It is better for us for the future to go and carry on business ourselves on our own account out of that which Grace has provided for us; that it may in be our power and choice to give to God out of that which he gives us without fear of others." And so after twenty years they returned to


[p. 578] {#po.18.p578}


[p. 579] {#po.18.p579}
ܗܦܟܘ ܠܥܡܝܕܐ. ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܐ̱ܚܘ̈ܬܐ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܝܢ ܒܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܢܘܝܘܬܐ ܘܕܩܕܝܫܘܬܐ ܒܬܘܠܐܝܬ. ܡܢ ܛܠܝܘܬܗܝܢ ܡܬܕܒܪܢ ܗ̈ܘܝ. ܘܒܟܝܐ ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܘܒܥܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܨܠܘܬܐ ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ ܐܡܝܢܢ ܗܘܝ. ܘܟܕ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܝܗܘܢ ܐܒܝܠܢ ܗ̈ܘܝ. ܒܙܒܢܐ ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܚܙܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܫܡܥܢ ܗܘܝ ܒܙܒܢ ܙܒܢ ܕܩܝܡܝܢ ܘܒܐܬܪ ܦܪ̈ܣܝܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܘܟܕ ܐܬܘ ܠܐ ܐܫܬܘܕܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܢܘܓܪܐ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܗܢܘܢ ܒܕܩܘ ܠܗܝܢ. ܐܬܚܫܒܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܒܘܪܟܬܐ ܐܦ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܢܬܬܓܪܘܢ. ܟܠ ܝܘ̈ܡܝ ܚܝܝܗܘܢ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܫܩܠܘ ܘܐܙܠܘ ܝܬܒܘ ܩܘܝܐܝܬ ܐܪ̈ܒܥܬܝܗܘܢ ܒܡܠܝܛܝܢܐ ܡܕܝܢܬܐ ܕܩܦܕܘܩܝܐ. ܘܐܡܢ ܡܟܝܠ ܫܪܝܘ ܒܥܡܠܐ ܪܘܚܢܐܝܬ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܥܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ. ܡܦܠܓܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܠܫܪܟܐ ܕܐܟܣ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܡܣܟܢܐ. ܘܥܡ ܫܘܪܝܗܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܗܝ ܕܐܦ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܡܬܬܢܝܚ ܗܘܐ: ܘܩܘܒܠ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܘܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܕܡ̈ܣܟܢܐ ܘܕܣ̈ܢܝܩܐ ܒܟܠ ܝܘܡ ܡܩܒܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܬܘܒ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܟܠܐ ܘܥܠܘܒܘܬܐ ܘܕܓܠܘܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܥܛܪܐ ܕܒܣܡܐ: ܠܘܬ ܝܕܥ ܟܠ ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ
their country i. e. to Amida. Now they had two sisters who also had been engaged in practices of asceticism and sanctity in virgin fashion from their childhood, and at all times constantly devoted themselves to sorrow and tears and petitions and prayers, and that while they were mourning over these same brothers during so long a time in which they had not seen them, but had only heard from time to time that they were alive and in the land of the Persians; and when they came they did not recognise them on account of the lapse of time, until they themselves informed them. And they determined to make a gain of their sisters' spiritual blessing also all the days of their life; and so they removed and went and settled the four of them together at Melitene a city of Cappadocia, and there they thenceforth began to labour in spiritual fashion at their business, and all the income that they received they used to divide equally between themselves and the strangers and poor. And from the time when they began, because God also was pleased with them, they both received the gratitude and the blessings of the poor and needy every day, and further the business carried on without deceit and extortion and lying ascended like the vapour of incense at all times from the devout men themselves to him who knows all, and so according to the riches of his mercy he
ܕܢܟ̈ܦܐ ܣܠܝܩ ܗܘܐ: ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܪܚܡܘܗܝ ܗܘ ܕܠܐ ܫܒܩ ܐܘ ܫܒܝܩ ܠܟܠ ܕܬܟܝܠܝܢ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܚܘܐ ܗܘܐ. ܘܒܘܪܟܬܗ ܒܟܠ ܕܥܒܕܝܢ ܪܕܝܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܠܥܘܬܪܐ ܪܒܐ ܐܬܘܢ ܟܕ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܬܡܝܗܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܘܡܬܕܡܪܝܢ. ܕܟܕ ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ ܡܡܒܕܪܝܢ ܠܗ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܛܒܐ ܕܚܝܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܘܕܙܕܩܬܗܘܢ ܒܟܠ ܕܘܟ ܢܦܩ ܗܘܐ. ܘܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܦܢ̈ܝܢ. ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܨܐܝܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܣܘ̈ܢܩܢܝܗܘܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܠܐܢܫ ܐܢܫ ܐܝܟ ܙܕܩܗ ܡܡܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܬܘܒ ܗܢܘܢ ܠܣܥܘܪܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܕܝܦܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܟܠ ܕܘܟ ܫܡܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܣܢܝܩܝܢ. ܡܒܝܐܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܫܕ ܡܘܗ̈ܒܬܗܘܢ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܒܬܐܓܘܪܬܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܐܗܡܝܘ ܗܘܘ. ܘܒܗܝ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܟܫܝܪܐܝܬ ܘܚܦܝܛܐܝܬ ܒܥܝܪܘܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܙܘܛܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܐܚܘ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܛܘܒ̈ܢܝܬܐ. ܗܝ ܟܕ ܗܝ ܥܠ ܬܐܓܘܪܬܐ ܕܒܫܕ ܡܘܗ̈ܒܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܡܠܗܘܢ ܡܚܦܛܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗܝܢ. ܕܐܦ ܗܢܝܢ ܗܝ ܗܕܐ ܥܠ ܫܪܟܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܝܗܝܢ ܢܘ̈ܣܦܢ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܨܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܓܒܗ ܝܗܒ ܗܘܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܣܓܝܐ ܗܘܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܬܡܢ ܓܠܝܐܝܬ ܒܥܒܕܐ. ܗܝ ܕܒܥܒܫܐ ܐܬܐܡܪܬ ܡܫܬܡܠܝܐ ܗܘܬ. ܕܐܣܝܡ ܠܟܘܢ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܥܕܡܐ
who has not deserted and does not desert any who trust in him showed his favour openly, and his blessing rested on all that they did. And thus ina short time they attained to great wealth, while even they themselves marvelled and wondered how it was that, while they were always scattering it, it increased with them. And thenceforth the fame of their kindness and of their almsgiving went out everywhere, and needy persons from all quarters used to come to them, and supply their needs from them for each man [according to his due; and moreover the visitation of the persecuted whom they heard to be in need everywhere they would console through their gifts. And accordingly they disregarded the worldly trade, and strenuously and earnestly followed the spiritual one with great watchfulness, insomuch that they would press the same upon their blessed sisters with regard to the trade carried on through the gifts derived from their labour, that they also might add this to the rest of their practices. And thus each would on his part give as he was able, and still it increased the more, so that in that place was manifestly fulfilled that which was said in the prophet, "I will pour blessings upon you till ye shall say Enough" Thus therefore it happened to these men from


[p. 580] {#po.18.p580}
ܕܬܐܡܪܘܢ ܟܕܘ ܗܟܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܢܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܓܕ̈ܫܐ ܗܘܬ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܐ ܢܣܦܩܘܢ ܠܡܦܩܘ ܘܠܡܬܠ. ܟܕ ܐܟܣܢܕܘܟܝܘܢ ܒܝܬܗܘܢ ܥܒܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܠܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܘܠܫܪܟܐ ܕܣܢܝ̈ܩܐ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܐܟܠܘܣ ܕܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܒܒܝܬܐ ܗܘ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܘ: ܕܥܒ̈ܝܕܐ ܘܐܓܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܡܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܘܟܪܟܐ ܕܥܝ̈ܠܕܘܠܐ ܘܕܐܟܣܢܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܟܢܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܐܦ ܛܠܝ̈ܬܐ. ܘܣܒ̈ܬܐ. ܘܣܡܝܐ ܘܣܓܝܦܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܒܬܛܟܣܐ ܕܕܝܪܝܘܬܐ ܒܟܘܠܗܘܢ ܗܘ̈ܦܟܐ ܕܒܝܬܗܘܢ ܢܬܕܒܪܘܢ. ܒܨܘܡܐ ܘܢܙܝܪܘܬܐ ܘܥܢܘܝܘܬܐ. ܘܫܒ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܕܝܪܐ ܗ̈ܘܝܢ ܗ̈ܘܝ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܦܠܚܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܩܝ̈ܠܝܬܐ ܡܟܪ̈ܫܬܐ ܠܨܠܘܬܐ ܘܠܥܡܠܐ ܟܣ̈ܝܐ ܕܠܠܝܘܬܐ. ܕܟܠ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܫܪܘܪ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܝܨܠܐ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܡܢ ܟ̈ܠܝܬܗܘܢ ܠܠܝܐ ܟܠܗ. ܙܘܡܪܐ ܪܘܚܢܝܐ ܘܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܟܬܒ̈ܐ. ܡܫܬܡܥ ܗܘܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܘܒܨܦܪܐ ܬܘܒ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܫܠܝܡܐ ܗܘܬ ܬܫܡܫܝܬܐ ܕܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ. ܠܐ ܡܩܒܠ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܦܘܩ ܣܟ ܘܢܥܒܕ ܐܦܘܩܪܝܣܝܣ ܠܐܢܫ. ܠܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܩܫܝܫ ܕܫܡܗ ܐܠܝܐ ܥܨܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ. ܕܡܛܠ ܐܣܦܠܚܝܐ ܕܒܝܬܐ ܒܣܒ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܘܐܬܥܒܨܝ. ܘܗܘ ܒܠܚܘܕܘܗܝ ܫܩܠ ܐܢܬܬܐ. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܠܘܬܗ ܡܐܠܠܬ ܟܬܒܐ ܐܫܬܡܠܝܬ ܗܝ ܕܐܡܪܐ: ܕܐܢܬܬܐ ܛܒܬܐ ܬܬܝܪܗܒ ܠܓܒܪܐ ܕܕܡܠ ܡܢ ܡܪܝܐ. ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܐܢܬܬܐ
God, insomuch that they were unequal to the task of bringing forth and giving, while their house was turned into a hospice (ξενοδοχεῖον) for strangers and other persons in need. And accordingly there were a multitude (ὄχλος) of many persons in that house, of slaves and hired men and bondmaids, and travellers besides and strangers who were gathered together for God's sake, and girls also, and old women, and blind and maimed men, insomuch that they practised the ordinances of monasticism in all the departments of their house, fasting and abstinence and asceticism; and they had weeks as ina monastery and did menial work, and separate cells for prayers and for secret labours by night, since each of them used to watch and pray. And accordingly all the night there was heard from their cells spiritual singing and the reading of the Scriptures; and so in the morning also as well, until each one's repetition of the service was finished, he would not consent to go out at all and make an answer (ἀπόκρισις) to anyone's call. But the elder whose name was Elijah they would urge, for the sake of the security (ἀσφάλεια) of the house, to take a wife; and he yielded to the pressure, and he alone took a wife, while with him also the Scripture-saying was fulfilled which says, "A


[p. 581] {#po.18.p581}


[p. 582] {#po.18.p582}
ܗܕܐ ܟܕ ܥܠܬ ܘܚܙܬ ܕܚܦܝܛܝܢ ܠܡܪܗܛ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ. ܫܪܝܬ ܘܐܩܝܠܬ ܪܗܛܐ. ܘܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܥܒܪܬ. ܒܚܫܐ ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܘܨܘܡܐ ܘܟܗܪܐ ܘܨܠܘܬܐ. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܝܠܝܦܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܠܕܝܪܐ ܐܘ ܠܡܕܒܪܐ ܡܬܥܬܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܬܦܘܩ. ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܕܝܢ ܒܢ̈ܝܐ. ܥܡ ܒܪܬܐ ܚܕܐ. ܘܡܚܕܐ ܠܕܒܕܬܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܕܝܪܝܬܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ. ܒܬܪ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܪܗܛܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܓܕܫ ܘܐܢܢܩ̈ܘܣ ܕܐܦܘܩܪܝܣܝܣ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܒܠܠܝܐ ܢܣܥܪܘܢ. ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܪܡܫܐ ܡܬܓܕܒ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܬܬܣܝܡ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܨܒܘܬܐ ܗܿܝܢ. ܟܕ ܫܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܒܦܝܣܐ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܫܪܟܐ ܕܢܦܝܣܘܢ ܚܠܝܦܘܗܝ ܠܡܪܝܐ. ܚܠܦ ܗܝ ܠܡ ܕܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܗܝ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ̇ ܦܘܡܐ ܝܗܒ ܠܝ ܘܠܫܢܐ ܡܒܛܠ ܐܢܐ. ܨܠܘ ܥܠܝ ܕܢܦܨܝܢܝ ܐܠܗܐ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܢܬܛܪܛܫ ܒܝ ܚܛܗܐ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܣܥܪ ܐܢܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܨܒܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܣܥܪ ܗܘܐ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܫܡܥܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܝܬܪܘܬܗܘܢ. ܐܡܬܝ ܕܒܠܠ̈ܬܐ ܠܡܕܝܢܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܙܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܠܢ. ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܥܪܝܢܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܥܒܕܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܘܓܕܫ ܕܐܦ
good woman shall be given to a man who feareth the Lord" For this woman, when she came in and saw that they were bent on running in the path of righteousness, began and quickened the running, and passed them all, in sorrow and tears and fasting and watching and prayers, while she also learned the psalms by heart; and she was further prepared to go out to a monastery or to the desert. And she had sons born to her beside one daughter; and she at once made her a nun. And so all these excellent persons ran after spiritual things. But, if it happened that cases of necessity (ἀνάγκας) arose for them to make an answer (ἀπόκρισις) to a call during the night, one of them would be chosen and appointed the evening before to perform that office, while he would make entreaty and request all the others to entreat the Lord for him 'because | am neglecting the praise of God, for the sake of which he gave me a mouth and a tongue Pray for me that God may preserve me, lest a stain of sin be secretly formed in me through the things that | am doing’; and thus he would carry out that office; so that on account of the fame of their excellent mode of life, when occasions called us to the royal city we used to make our lodging with them, and it might happen that we even passed several days in their house both on the


[p. 583] {#po.18.p583}
ܝܘܡܬܐ ܝܕܝ̈ܥܐ ܘܒܡܣܩܐ ܘܒܡܚܬܬܐ. ܒܚܒܝܫܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܠܒܕܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܟܕ ܡܟܝܠ. ܒܝܬ ܣܗ̈ܕܐ ܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܒܝܬܐ ܕܡܥܡܪܗܘܢ ܥܒܕܘ ܗܘܘ. ܘܥܕܢܐ ܕܟܢܘܫܝܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܕܠܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܬܡܢܟ ܡܬܘܥܕܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܐܢܫ ܕܝܕܝܥ ܘܝܩܝܪ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܬܡܢ ܡܩܒܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܟܕ ܒܐܢܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܫܡܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܥܠܡܐ ܡܬܩܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܒܝ̈ܛܟܣܝܗܘܢ ܘܒܢ̈ܡܘܣܝܗܘܢ ܚܝܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܕܪ̈ܘܪܒܐ ܘܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܒܒ̈ܘܣܐ ܘܐܓܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܥܒܕܐ ܘܐܡ̈ܗܬܐ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܒܢܟܦܘܬܐ ܘܫܘܒܚܐ ܘܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܬܗܦܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܒܨܘܡܐ ܘܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ. ܢܦܢܢ ܒܝܢܝܢ ܘܠܢ ܒܣܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܟܕ ܘܝܐ ܠܚܝܝܢ ܡܛܦܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܕܫܡܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܟܪ̈ܣܛܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܣܝܡ ܥܠܝܢ. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܒܚܫܐ ܡܬܕܟܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܕܐܡܪ. ܕܡܟ̈ܣܐ ܘܙܢܝ̈ܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܢ ܠܟܘܢ ܠܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܒܬܡܗܐ ܕܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܬܗܪܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܒܪܒܘܬ ܚܘܒܗܘܢ ܘܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ. ܕܟܕ ܢܚܙܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܢ ܪ̈ܘܪܒܐ ܘܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܟܡܐ ܕܡܨܝܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܠܘܬܢ. ܐܠܐ ܟܕ ܣܒܪܝܢ ܥܠܝܢ ܕܒܫܪܪܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܐܦ ܟܪ̈ܣܛܝܢܐ. ܚܕ ܚܕ ܕܝܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܥܠ ܚܘܫܒ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܥܠ ܫܪܟܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܡܢܢ
way up and on the way back, since they had now made a martyrs' chapel in one of the chambers of their dwelling, and at the hour of their own assembly for service they used to meet there, and anyone who was known and honoured by them they would receive there. Accordingly, when we looked at these persons who were called by the name of laymen, and at their regulations and customs (νόμος), how great and small down to little children and hired servants and slaves male and female passed their time in devout practices and rendering of praise and thanks to God, and in fasting and prayers at all times, we inwardly despised ourselves, crying woe upon our life, since the Christian name only without deeds was called over us, while we also remembered with sorrow the words of our Lord who says, "The tax-gatherers and the harlots go into the kingdom of heaven before you" And so we marvelled at these things while we were with them; and we were especially astonished at the greatness of their kindness and the direction of their will toward God, so that, whenever they saw us great and small, they would thus with as little intermission as possible refuse to leave us, but, thinking of us that we were in truth actually Christians, each of them would


[p. 584] {#po.18.p584}
ܫܐܠ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܫܡܥ. ܘܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܙܕܩ ܠܝ ܠܡ ܕܦܠܢ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܐܬܕܒܪ. ܘܕܐܝܢܐ ܠܡ ܥܡܠܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܩܪܒ ܠܝ. ܟܠ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܦܪܘܫܐܝܬ. ܐܝܟ ܛܟܣܗ. ܘܐܢ ܢܫ̈ܐ ܘܐܢ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ. ܘܚܢܢ ܐܝܟ ܗܝ ܕܐܦ ܡܪܢ ܐܡܪ ܠܣܦܪ̈ܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܝܫܐ. ܕܐܣܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܡ ܡܘܒܠܐ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܥܠ ܨܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝܢܫܐ. ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܘܐܦ ܠܐ ܒܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܨܒ̈ܥܬܟܘܢ ܩܪܒܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܗܝܢ. ܗܟܢܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܚܢܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܒܢܚܦܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ܘܒܬܦ̈ܫܝܬܐ ܕܐܒܗܬܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܚܦܛܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ. ܘܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ. ܒܦܘܠܚܢܗܘܢ ܪܬܚܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܥܡ ܟܠܗܘܢ. ܚܕ ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ. ܐܝܟ ܡܢ ܕܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܝܘܬܪܢܐ ܕܬܐܓܘܪܬܐ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܟܠܝܘܡ ܨܝܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ. ܣܕܡܘ ܒܪܥܝܢܗܘܢ ܕܢܣܝܒ ܡܕܡ ܕܡܛܝܒ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܢܦܘܩ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܦܐ ܘܡܛܪ̈ܦܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܥܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܢܦܠܓ ܠܗܘܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܬܗ. ܘܢܬܬܓܪ ܗܘ ܪܘܚܢܐܝܬ ܒܚܕ ܨܠܘܬܗܘܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܙܒܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܪ̈ܕܝܦܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܗ̈ܝܡܢܐ. ܘܡܬܛܪܦܝܢ ܒܟܠ ܐܬܪ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܢܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܬܟܪܟ ܒܟܠܗ ܣܘܪܝܐ. ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܥܪܒ ܘܠܩܠܝܢܝܩܣ ܘܠܡܕܒܪܐ. ܥܡ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܛܠܝܐ. ܘܒܐܣܟܡܐ ܕܬܓܪܐ ܥܠܡܢܝܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܝܕܝܥ. ܘܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܡܬܬܓܪ
ask to hear from us about his thoughts and about the other modes of life, and "How must | deal with such-and-such a thought?" and "What labour will bring me near to God?", each of them separately according to his station, whether women or men And we like what our Lord said also to the scribes and Pharisees, "Ye bind heavy burdens upon men's necks, but ye yourselves do not touch them not even with one of your fingers" similarly would urge them to things to which we were ourselves strangers by describing a model of righteousness and by stories of the fathers. And they all vied with one another the more in the ardour with which they carried out their observances. But, inasmuch as the profit of the spiritual trade in those who flocked to them every day was not enough for them, they resolved that one of these excellent brothers should take what God had provided for them, and go out to the districts in which the persecuted and ill-used convents were dwelling, and distribute his bodily things among them, and himself make spiritual gain through their prayers; since the time had in fact come when the believers were persecuted and maltreated in every district. And so he would go out and go round the whole of Syria, and as far as ‘Arab and Callinicus and the desert, accompanied by two bondmen, and he did so in


[p. 585] {#po.18.p585}
ܗܘܐ ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܢܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܬܕܡܘܪܬܐ܂. ܡ̈ܘܒܠܐ ܪܘܪ̈ܒܬܐ ܒܪܚܡܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܬܐܓܘܪܬܗܘܢ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܒܟܠ ܝܘܡ ܡܥܕܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܝܘ̈ܡܝ ܚܝ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܒܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܦ ܠܬܘܢܝܐ ܕܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܠܒܪܝܢ.. ܒܪܚܘܒܬ ܚ̈ܝܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ܂ ܫܠܡܘܢ. ܟܕ ܐܦ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܡܢ ܪܒܘܬܐ ܕܬܫܒܘܚܬܗܘܢ ܣܦܝܩܝܢ ܠܡܟܬܒܬܢܫܘ. ܒܚܪܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܘܐܦ ܟܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܙܒܢܘܢ. ܘܕܪ̈ܐܗܘܢ ܘܫܪܟܐ ܕܐܨܒܘܬܐ ܥܠܡ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܕܩܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ.܆ ܘܐܦܠܓܘܢ ܠܡܣܟܢܐ ܘܠܕܝܪ̈ܝܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ܂ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܝܪ̈ܝܐ. ܟܕ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܠܝܐ ܩܕܡ ܕܢܣܒ ܐܣܟܡܐ ܢܦܩ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ. ܐܢܬܬܗ ܕܝܢ ܘܒܪܬܗܼ ܒܕܝܪܐ ܕܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܒܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܡܒܘܓ ܫܠܡܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܝܗܝܢ. ܐܚܘ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܘܐܦ ܥܕ ܗܫܐ.. ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܫܢܬ ܬܡܢܡܐܐ ܘܫܒܥܝܢ ܘܬܡܢܐ. ܒܐܢܙܝܛ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܐܘܪ̈ܛܝܐ ܚܝ̈ܝܢ ܘܠܒܫ̈ܢ ܕܝܪܐ ܒܩܪܝܬܐ ܕܢܣ. ܘܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܒܣܝܒܘܬܐ ܥܡܝܩܬܐ.. ܘܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܓܡܝܪܘܬܐ܀
the dress (σχῆμα) of a secular trader without being known, and he was making spiritual gains. And so by the love of God which they showed in their trade these admirable men would every day send great loads to heaven, and completed the days of their life in practices which it surpasses even the power of words to describe, in love of the new life of the kingdom of heaven, while we have not been competent to describe any part of the greatness of their glory. But at last they even sold all that they had also, their mansions and the rest of the worldly goods that they possessed, and distributed it among the poor and the monks; and so they all became monks. The blessed Elijah had departed from the world before taking the habit (σχῆμα); but his wife and daughter ended their lives in a women's monastery in the territory of Mabbug and their sisters are even down to the present time also, which is the year eight hundred and seventy-eight living in Anzetene the country of the Urtaye and they preside over a monastery in the village of N'ns, and they are in extreme old age, and occupied in labours of perfection
ܫܠܡܬ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܐܚ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܠܝܐ ܘܬܐܘܕܘܪܐ ܬܓܪ̈ܐ܀
The history of two brothers Elijah and Theodore the traders is ended.


[p. 586] {#po.18.p586}
ܬܘܒ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܬܪܬܝܢ܆ ܕܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܕܐܬܬܚܕ ܒܓܘܪܓܐ ܕܢܣܝܘܢܐ ܕܓܢܒܐ ܘܒܬܪܟܢ ܐܬܐ ܠܬܝܒܘܬܐ܀ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܚܕ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܝܚܝܕܐܝܬ ܒܐܢܬܛܘܢ ܕܐܠܟܣܢܕܪܝܐ܂. ܥܡ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܝܬܒ ܗܘܐ܂ ܡܢ ܙܒܢܐ ܝܕܝܥܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܠܬܡܢ ܡܛܝܢ ܘܥܠܢ ܠܘܬܗ ܘܐܬܒܪܟܢ ܡܢܗ.. ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܬܠܬܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܠܘܬܗ ܥܒܕܪܢ.. ܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܝܕܝ̈ܥܐ ܕܬܡܢ. ܠܘܬܗ ܐܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܣܝܒܪ ܠܗ ܕܒܥܐ ܕܢܥܡܪ ܠܘܬܗ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܒܐܚܐ ܗܘ ܒܥܘܡܪܗ ܡܬܦܣ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܗ. ܘܟܕ ܥܠ ܘܥܒܕ ܠܘܬܗ ܐܝܟ ܫܒܬܐ ܚܕܐ ܐܪܡܝ ܒܠܒܗ ܣܛܢܐ.. ܕܢܣܕܒ ܡ̈ܐܢܝ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܕܗܘ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ, ܘܢܥܪܘܩ. ܘܟܕ ܥܝܢ ܘܝܕܥ ܟܘܠ ܡܕܡ ܘܐܬܦܪܣ ܒܚܘ̈ܫܒܘܗܝ ܕܡܢܐ ܢܣܥܘܪ.. ܩܡ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܟܢܫ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܐܫܟܚ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܩܕ̈ܝܫܐ ܘܟ̈ܬܒܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܘ ܠܐ ܙܥܘܪ. ܘܦܩܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܛܥܘܢܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ܂ ܘܥܪܩ. ܘܐܙܠ ܐܫܟܚ ܓܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܥܐܠܝܢ ܠܡܕܒܪܐ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܠܘܒܐ ܕܡܬܩܪܐ܂ ܩܠܝܐ. ܘܝܗܒ
Next the thirty-second history, of a certain brother who was caught by the enticement of temptation to theft and afterwards came to repentance. A certain blessed man had been settled for a considerable time as a solitary in a cell in the Ennaton of Alexandria with two disciples; and, when we ourselves arrived at that place and went in to him and received his blessing, three days after we had departed from his presence a brother from one of the well-known convents of the place came to him under the plea of desiring to live with him, even as | had myself made the acquaintance of that brother in his convent; and, when he had come in and passed about one week with him, Satan put it into his heart to take the contents of that solitary's cell and flee. And, having looked and learned everything, and devised in his mind a plan of action, he rose by night and collected everything that he could (since that blessed man had indeed no small number of saints and books), and he took them, a great load, and fled. And he went and found camel-drivers going into the desert above Libya that is called Cellia and he gave them a fee, leading them to think that he was carrying his own


[p. 587] {#po.18.p587}
ܠܗܘܢ ܐܓܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܣܝܒܪ ܠܗܘܢ܇. ܕܡ̈ܐܢܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܫܩܝܠ ܕܢܐܙܠ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܬܡܢ. ܘܕܒܪܘܗܝ܂ ܘܥܠ ܥܡܗܘܢ. ܘܥܠ ܠܬܡܢ ܠܘܬ ܐܢܫ ܘܒܥܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܩܠܝܬܐ ܕܢܥܡܪ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܥܒܕ ܬܡܢ ܫܒܬܐ ܚܕܐ.. ܕܒܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܚܘܫܒܘ̈ܗܝ.. ܘܡܢܓܕ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܒܗ ܘܐܡܪ ܒܝܢܘܗܝ ܠܢܦܫܗ. ܐܝܟ ܕܗܘ ܟܕ ܒܟܐ ܡܒܕܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܢ ܥܠ ܟܠܗܝܢ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܡ ܐܘ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܘܝܐ ܨܒܝܬ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ. ܠܟܪܝܣܛܘܣ܂ ܟܫ ܐܘ ܠܣܛܢܐ. ܗܐ ܓܝܪ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ܂ ܕܣܛܢܐ ܦܠܚܬ. ܘܨܒܝܢܐ ܕܕܝܘܐ ܓܡܪܬ܂ ܘܦܡܠܝܬ. ܘܢܦܫܐ ܕܨܠܡܐ ܒܕܡܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ܂ ܐܟܝܬ. ܘܠܐܘܬܐ ܘܕܘܥܝܬܐܼ ܕܥܡܠܗ ܕܣܒܐ ܗܘ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܐܠܝܨܬ. ܘܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܥܠܝܟ. ܒܟܠ ܥܕܢ̈ܝܢ ܡܬܬܢܚ ܘܓܥܐ. ܘܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܣܒܪܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܕܦܘܪܩܢܐ. ܗܝ ܕܝܢ ܠܡ ܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܬܘܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܘܬܘܝܪ ܘܒܟܐ܂. ܠܒܕܬ ܫܒܬܐ ܚܕܐ.. ܟܕ ܡܒܟܐ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܝ. ܘܡܬܚܫܒ ܐܢܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܘܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ܂ ܕܡܢܐ ܓܕܦܢܝ. ܡܢ ܐܛܥܝܢܝ ܒܚܪ ܡܢܐ ܐܥܒܕ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܒܟܐ ܠܢܦܫܝ. ܘܝ ܠܝ ܕܚܒܪ̈ܝ ܓܢܒܝܢ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܒܟܣܝܐ ܂ ܘܐܢܐ܂ ܓܢܒܬ ܠܝ ܬܫܢܝܩܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܡܢܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܡܢܝ ܟܕ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܥܬܝܕ ܠܝ ܕܐܙܠ. ܐܚܕܐ ܐܦܘܠܘܓܝܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܬܠ ܚܠܦ ܪܘܦܥܐ ܗܢܐ ܘܟܕ ܠܡ ܐܢܐ ܡܒܟܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܢܦܫܝ.
belongings in order to go thither. And they took him, and he went into the desert with them; and he went in to a certain man there, and made search for a cell for himself in which to live and be a solitary. And, when he had passed one week there, his thoughts began to pass judgment upon him, and his heart to smite him, and he would say to himself, as he himself would inform us about everything, weeping: "In what way, O wretched man, did you wish to become a solitary? To Christ, pray, or to Satan? Lo! you have performed the service of Satan, and executed and fulfilled the will of a demon, and afflicted a soul fashioned in the divine likeness, and carried off the toil and sweat of that holy old man's labour; and he is sobbing and crying before God on your account at all times. And how have | hope of life and salvation?’ Then" (he would say) "| passed one week in penitence and horror and tears, weeping for myself, and meditating upon these things and saying, 'What has befallen me? Who led me astray? What shall | do? How shall | weep for myself? Alas for me! since my companions are secretly stealing a kingdom, and | stole eternal torment for myself. What will happen to me when | am about to go to God? What defence (ἀπολογία) have | to give for this wickedness?’ And, while | on my


[p. 588] {#po.18.p588}
ܗܠܝܢ ܕܫܡܿܥܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܝ ܡܬܕܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܕܢܐܠܦܘܢ ܥܠܝܼܬܐ ܕܐܒܠܐ ܘܕܒܟܝܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܚܦܝܛܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܗܳܝܕܝܢ ܠܡ ܓܠܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܬܡܗܘ. ܘܐܡܿܪܬ ܠܗܘܢ. ܕܡܢܐ ܡܿܠܟܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܝ ܕܐܥܒܕ. ܡܛܠ ܕܚܘܫܒܝ ܡܓܪܓ ܠܝ ܕܐܚܢܘܩ ܢܦܫܝ. ܘܐܹܒܝܼܕ ܡܢ ܚܿܝ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ. ܕܕܓܠܘܢ ܒܝ ܘܓܪܓܘܢܝ ܥܠ ܣܘܪܚܢܐ ܗܢܐ. ܘܗܢܘܢ ܥܡܕ ܩܕ̈ܝܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܿܠܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܝ܂. ܕܐܗܼܦܟ ܓܢܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܡܕ̈ܝܙܘܿ. ܟܕ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܝ ܂. ܕܐܢ! ܫܡܿܥܬ ܠܢ ܘܡܗܦܟ ܐܢܬ ܘܡܬܡܟܟ ܐܢܬ ܘܟܐܠ ܐܢܬ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܥܠ ܐܦܫ̈ ܣܟܠܘܬܟ ܗܕܐ.. ܘܐܠܗܐ ܡܪܚܡ ܥܠܝܟ܂ ܘܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܢܫܐ ܫܒ̈ܩܝܢ ܠܟ. ܘܐܦ ܗܢܝܼܢ ܡܪܚܡܝܢ ܥܠܝܟ܂ ܘܦܝܒܩܚܝܢ ܠܟ. ܘܐܼܦ ܒܥܝܢ ܚܠܦܝܟ. ܘܟܕ ܠܡ ܥܨܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܝ ܐܢܐ ܒܒܪܘܐܬܐ ܕܐܦ̈ܝ ܡܬܬܚܕ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ. ܘܡܬܥܘܟ ܗܿܘܝܬ ܡܢ ܕܗܕܐ ܐܣܥܘܪ. ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܗܘܢ.. ܕܒܪܘ̈ܝܬܢ ܐܦ̈ܝ ܕܗܕܐ ܐܥܒܕ. ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܐ'ܦܐ.. ܐܘ ܒܐܝܢܐ ܟܪܘܡܐ܂. ܐܘ ܒܐܝܢܐ ܒܝܬ ܥܫ̈ܢܐ ܕܢܚܫܐ ܡܨܝܐ ܗܕܐ ܠܡܫܬܡܫܘ. ܐܡܿܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܝ ܓܝܪ ܠܡ ܗܢܘܢ ܛܘܒܢ̈ܐ܂. ܕܡܢܐ ܦܩܚ ܠܟ.
side was weeping for myself, my hearers were wondering, and were desirous of learning the cause of the lamentation and weeping on my part. Then | revealed it to them, and they marvelled. And | said to them: 'What do you advise me to do? because my meditation is inciting me to strangle myself and perish out of this life, which deceived me and incited me to this crime’. And these saints advised me to return the stolen goods to their owners, saying to me, 'If you listen to us and make restitution and humble yourself and ask mercy for this your offence, God will be merciful to you and men also will forgive you; and even they themselves will be merciful to you and forgive you, and will also make petition for you’. And, while they pressed me, | on my part was restrained by my shamefacedness and hindered from doing this, saying to them: 'My face is ashamed to do this. With what face, or with what colour (χρῶμα), or with what brazen front can this be carried out?' But the blessed men went on saying to me, ‘Which is better for you, to endure reproach and transitory shame here, and repent and live while you have opportunity, while you further relieve the souls to which you have given sorrow and bitterness, or to be consumed in the
ܕܗܪܟܐ ܬܣܝܒܪ ܚܣܕܐ ܘܒܪܘܬܬܐ ܥܒܘܪܬܐ: ܘܬܬܘܒ ܘܬܐܚܐ ܥܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܐܬܪܐ: ܟܕ ܬܘܒ ܬܢܫܝܝ ܠܢܦܫ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܟܐܒܬ ܘܐܡܼܪܪܬ ܐܸܢܝ̈ܢ.. ܐܘ ܒܗܽܘ ܬܟܢܝܩܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܬܬܛܠܩ܆. ܐܝܟܐܼ ܕܒܙܘܐܬܐܼ ܘܚܣܕܐ ܘܐܫܢܝܩܐ ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ ܠܐ ܓܡܡܪܝܢܼ ܘܠܐ ܛܠܩܝܢ܆. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܗܘܠܝܢܿ ܠܡ ܡܢ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܠܝ ܐܬܡܠܠ. ܩܡܿܬ ܫܒܝܩܬ ܡܐܢܐ ܘܟ̈ܬܒܐ ܘܣܗ̈ܕܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܛܒܝܥܝܢ.܀ ܘܐܬܝܬ ܟܕ ܒܝܗܝܬ ܐܢܐ. ܘܗܐ ܝܬܝܒ ܐܢܐ ܗܪܟܐ܂. ܥܕ ܡܦܫܬܟܚܝܢ ܠܝ ܡܦ̈ܝܣܢܐ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܗ ܗܼܘ ܐܚܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܢ ܦܘܡܗ. ܒܡܘܟܟܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܘܒܕ̈ܡܥܐ ܐܫܚ̈ܕܬܐ܂ ܩܕܡܝ ܬܢܐ ܗܘܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܕ ܒܕ̈ܘܟܝܬܐ ܡܬܟܪܡ ܗܘܝܬ ܕܡܢ ܩܕ̈ܝܫܐ ܐܬܒܪܡ܂. ܐܫܟܚܬܗ ܒܕܝܪܐ ܚܕܐ ܕܡܬܩܪܝܼܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܐܢܕܪܝܐ܂. ܒܥܝ̱ܒܪܐ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܡܪܝ ܡܐܢܐ. ܕܡܬܩܪܐ܂ ܡܕܒܪܐ ܕܡܝܢܕܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܚܙܝܬܗܼ ܬܡܗܬ ܠܩܘܒܠܗ. ܘܐܠܬ ܫܠܡܗܼ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܦܣ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܐܝܠܼܝܢ ܕܓܝܼܕܫ. ܘܗܼܘ ܡܼܪܟܢ ܗܘܐ܂. ܘܟܡܝܪ ܘܕܗܼܐ. ܘܟܕ ܥܠ ܐܒܠܗ ܥܩܝܒܬܗ. ܐܝܬܒ ܩܿܛܪܓ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܒܝܿܕܩ ܥܠ ܣܘܪܚܢܗ. ܥܦ̈ܐ ܣܓܺܝܐܐ ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܪܫܡܬ. ܘܟܕ ܫܡܿܥܬ܂ ܬܡܗܬ ܒܡܬܬܘܝܢܘܬܗ ܕܓܒܪܐ. ܘܠܝܒܠܒܬܗ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪܬ ܠܗ. ܕܪܒ ܣܝܘܥܪܢܟ܂ ܘܕܬܗܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܘܝ܆. ܕܗܟܢܐ ܚܠܦ ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܟ ܐܡܝܼܣܪܬ ܥܠ ܒܪܘܬܬܐ:. ܘܩܝܒܝܠܬ ܥܠܝܟ
eternal torment, where shame and reproach and torment are not finished and brought to an end for ever and ever?’ When these words had been spoken to me by many men, | rose and left all the utensils and the books and the martyrs under seal, and | came in shame; and lo! | am settled here till intercessors are found for me". All these things about himself that brother was relating by word of mouth in my presence with great humility and copious tears; since, while | was going round the districts to be blessed by the saints, | found him in a monastery called that of father Andrew on the farther side where the holy Mar Menas is, which is called the desert of Mendia and, when | saw him, | was astonished in his presence, and | greeted him, having no knowledge of what had happened; and he on his side was bowed down, and was melancholy and wretched. And on my inquiring of him about his sorrow he sat down and accused himself and told the story of his crime at a length many times greater than what | have put in writing. And, when | heard it, | admired the man's penitence; and | comforted him by saying to him: "You have done a great deed and one that deserves admiration, in that for the sake of your soul's healing you have despised shame and have taken upon yourself that, be it ignominy


[p. 589] {#po.18.p589}


[p. 590] {#po.18.p590}
ܕܐܸܢ ܒܙܚܐ ܘܐܢ ܚܣܕܐ.. ܘܐܸܢ ܢܓܺܕܐ ܘܟܘܠ ܕܐܬܝܐ ܗܪܟܐ ܬܩܒܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܥܒܘܪ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ܇. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܬܬܐܦܠܛ ܡܢ ܗܽܘ ܢܓܕܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܘܕܚܝܼܝܠܐ ܕܒܓܗܢܐ ܕܡܿܡܟܬܪ ܠܥܠܡܟ ܘܗܦܐ ܠܐ ܬܕܚܠّ ܫܦܝܪ ܒܝܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܡܼܠܝܬ ܥܒܐܕܗ ܕܣܛܢܐ ܘܨܒܝܢܗ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܒܪ. ܐܦ ܐܸܢ ܫܘܪܥܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܥܒܝܼܕ ܠܟ ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܡܼܥ܂ ܐܬܠܒܝܼܒ. ܘܡܦܝܣ ܗܘܐ ܠܢ ܕܥܡܗ ܢܗܦܘܟ ܠܘܬ ܡܪܗ ܕܬܚܠܘܨܐ. ܕܢܦܝܣܗ ܕܢܩܒܠ ܕܝܠܗ ܟܠܝܼܡܐܝܬ܂ ܘܢܫܒܘܩ ܦܘܪܥܬܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܚܙܝܢܢ ܕܥܡܠܐ ܗܘ ܕܝܘܬܪܢܐ ܗܝ ܕܢܐܬܪ ܐܚܘܢ.. ܠܐ ܐܫܬܐܠܢܢ. ܘܬܘܒ ܘܗܿܝ ܕܡܢ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܡܬܬܒܥܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܕܢܥܒܕ܆. ܕܐܸܢ ܡܿܡܫܚܪ ܠܟ ܠܝܡ ܐܚܘܟ ܚܕ ܡܚܠܐ܇ ܙܠ ܥܡܝܗ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ. ܘܗܟܢܝܐ ܗܦ̱ܟܢܢ ܥܡܗܼ ܘܥܠܢ ܠܘܬ ܗܽܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ܂. ܟܕ ܠܗ ܫܒܩܢܢ ܒܪܘܚܩܐ. ܘܿܐܸܫܟܚܝܢܝܗܝ ܕܡܡܛܪܦ ܒܟܪܝܘܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܘܦܚܩܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ. ܡܛܠ ܣܗ̈ܕܐ ܘܡܛܘܠ ܟܬܝ̈ܬܒܐ ܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ. ܘܟܕ ܘܪܝܢܢ ܝܗ̇ܒܝܢܢ ܡܠܐ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ: ܕܐܸܢ ܡܫܟܚܝܢܢ! ܠܟ ܣܘܥܪܢܟ.. ܬܘܼܗܼ ܘܬܪܝ ܡܿܥܩܒ. ܗ̇ܚܙܝܢ ܚܢܢܟ ܫܐܠܬܐ ܐܥܢܢ ܡܢܗ ܕܢܥܝܼܒܕ ܠܢ.. ܘܢܫܬܟܚ
or reproach, or chastisements and everything that comes here, you will submit to them, they being things that are transitory, in order to escape from the severe and terrible chastisement in hell which lasts for ever. And now fear not. You did well in that you did not carry out the work of Satan and his will to the uttermost, though he caused you a fall inasmuch as you are a man. He on hearing these things was comforted; and he went on entreating us to return with him to the owner of the stolen property, in order to entreat him to receive his own in full and forgive the transgression. When we saw that it was a labour of profit to gain our brother we did not refuse; and further also we were required to fulfil the precept given in the command, "If thy brother shall force thee to go one mile, go with him two" And so we returned with him, and went in to that blessed man, having left him some distance away. And we found him agitated with great grief and much distress on account of the martyrs and on account of the books especially; and, when we began to speak before him, "Indeed we can find your property for you", he was astonished, and began to make inquiries. Then we on our part asked him to perform a request for us, and all his belongings would be found complete; and he on his side


[p. 591] {#po.18.p591}
ܥܒܕܗ ܟܠܗ ܡܫܡܠܝܐܝܬ. ܘܗܼܘ ܦܢܝ ܕܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܦܩܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܕܠܐ ܗܽܘ. ܐܢܐ ܥܬܝܕ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܫܠܡ. ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܩܪܒܢ ܚܢܢ ܠܗ ܬܝܒܘܬܐ. ܟܕ ܦܩܝܕܢܢ ܥܠ ܦܪܨܘܦܢ ܘܐܡܼܪܢܢ. ܕܡܛܠ ܕܣܛܢܐ ܐܒܝ̈ܐ ܐܚܬ ܒܢ̈ܫ ܐܢܫܐ ܐܪܥ ܠܢ: ܘܚܛܝ̣ܢܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܘܠܩܕܝܫܘܬܟ. ܩܒܝܼܠ ܕܝܠܟ. ܘܦܒܘܩ ܣܘܪܚܢܐ. ܘܟܬܪ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܥܡܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܡܫܠܡܢܐ ܕܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܕܫܒܘܩܘ ܘܢܫܬܒܩ ܠܟܘܢ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܣܓܝ ܝܩܝܼܪ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܘܐܬܬܥܝܩ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ. ܕܐܦܢ ܠܐ ܡܿܦܢܐ ܠܝ ܡܕܡ ܘܦܩܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܟܠ ܝܘ̈ܡܝ ܚܝܝ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܥܡܐ ܡܶܩܒܠ ܐܢܐ ܕܢܥܡܪ ܥܡܝ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܠܓܡܝܪ ܡܥܗܕ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܨܒܘܬܐ ܗܕܐ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܬܐ ܐܚܐ ܗܽܘ. ܟܕ ܡܬܝܒܟܐ. ܘܡܿܟܨܦܐ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܐܪܥܐ. ܘܡܿܩܛܪܔ ܢܦܫܗܼ ܩܫܝܐܝܬ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܿܕܡܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܡܢ ܣܢܐܗ ܢܬܩܛܪܓ. ܘܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܬܬܘܝܢܘܬܗ: ܘܟܠܢ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܐܬܕܡܪܢ ܛܒ ܘܬܡܗܢܢ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܦܬܚܬ ܬܪܥܝܬܗ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܢܬܬܘܼܐ ܘܢܣܥܘܪ: ܗܟܠ ܕܫܡܥܘ ܬܡܗܘ ܘܐܬܕܡܪܘ ܒܗܢ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܠܩܘܒܠ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܚܠܦ ܬܒܥܬܐ ܡܢ
answered "Everything that you command | for my part am ready to carry out even without that". Then we offered him repentance, having taken it upon ourself, and we said: "Since Satan, father, caused us to fall like men, and we sinned against God and your holiness, receive your own, and forgive the crime, and remain without ill-feeling, in order that you may be a fulfiller of the command which said ‘Forgive and it shall be forgiven unto you' But the blessed man was much grieved over him and was distressed, saying: "Even if he does not restore me anything and you command, all the days of my life | will consent that he shall live with me without ill-feeling, and | will never remind him of this matter". And so that brother came, repenting and weeping and with his face bowed down to the ground, and bitterly accusing himself, in such a way as he could not have been accused by one who hated him When that blessed man saw his penitence, and all of us alike greatly wondered and marvelled at the grace which had opened his heart and caused him thus to repent and to act in this way (and all who heard the story wondered and marvelled at it), then he in this way received thanks from everyone in place of punishment. And the blessed man received
ܟܘܠ ܐܢܫ ܐܫܬܗܝ. ܘܩܝܒܠܗ ܛܘܒܢܐ. ܘܐܪܡܝ ܠܗ ܬܝܒܘܬܐ. ܘܚܠܨܝ ܠܗ. ܘܐܓܪ ܠܗ ܚܡܪܐ. ܘܕܒܝܪ ܐܚܐ ܗܽܘ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܐܙܠ, ܘܟܠܗܘܢ ܡܐܢܝ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܝ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܛܥܐ, ܐܦ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܘܡܟܝܠ, ܐܝܟ ܣܝܪܘܚܐ ܡܬܘܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܣܟܠܘܬܗ. ܘܚܦܝܛ ܗܘܐ ܒܬܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܦܝܗ̇. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܚܢܢ ܒܘܠܝܢ ܫܒܩܢܝܗܝ. ܟܕ ܬܗܝܪܝܢܢ ܒܣܘܥܪܢܗ ܘܡܿܘܕܝܢܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ.
him, and laid a penance upon him, and absolved him. And he hired an ass for him; and that brother set out, and so he went and brought all the articles, not having forgotten anything. And thenceforth he continued to repent of his offence like a guilty man, and he was diligent in showing repentance for it, insomuch that we on our part left him in this condition, admiring what he had done and thanking God.


[p. 592] {#po.18.p592}
ܫܠܡܼܬ ܬܫܟܝܬܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܚܕ ܕܐܬܬܚܕ ܒܓܘܪܓܐ ܕܢܣܝܘܢܐ ܕܓܢܒܐ ܘܒܬܪܟܢ ܐܬܐ ܠܬܝܒܘܬܐ.
The history of a certain brother who was caught by the enticement of temptation to theft and afterwards came to repentance is ended.
ܬܘܒ. ܬܫܟܝܬܐ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܬܠܬ. ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܫܡܗ ܗܘܐ ܚܶܠܳܐ ܛܲܢܢܐ ܡܢ ܕܝܪܐ ܕܡܬܩܪܝܐ ܕܐܘܪ̈ܗܝܐ.
Next the thirty-third history, of a blessed man whose name was Hala the zealous from the monastery called that of the Edessenes.
ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܗܢܐ ܡܝܬܪܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܡܬܩܕܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܫܡܐ ܗܽܘ ܕܐܦ ܓܡܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܫܡܠܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ ܚܝܠܐ ܛܢܢܐ. ܗܢܐ ܕܒܫܪܪܐ ܛܢܢܐ ܘܡܟܝܟܐ ܘܥܢܘܝܐ ܘܡܠܦܢܐ. ܘܡܫܡܠܝܐ ܒܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܚܝܼܠܬ ܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܗܼܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܗܢܐ.
This excellent blessed man therefore was thus called by a name which was also completely fulfilled in him, Hala the zealous, a man who was in truth zealous and humble and ascetic and a teacher, and perfect in all the practices of religion. Now this blessed man lived in one of the convents of the Amidenes


[p. 593] {#po.18.p593}
ܒܝܕ ܡܢ ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܡ̈ܕܝܐ. ܕܡܬܩܪܐ ܕܐܘܪ̈ܗܝܐ. ܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܫܢ̈ܝܐ ܒܥܢܝܢܗ ܗܿܘܝܬ. ܟܕ ܒܟܠܗ̇ ܢܦܫܝ. ܡܠܚܐܝܬ ܬܗܝܪ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܝ̈ܘܡܝ ܩܪܝܒܘܬܝ ܕܠܘܬܗ. ܒܛܢܢܗ ܚܪܝܦܐ ܘܒܪܗܛܗ ܚܝܠܬܢܐ ܘܒܚܦܝܛܘܬܗ ܐܡܝܢܬܐ ܘܒܥܝܪܘܬܗ ܕܒܟܠ ܥܒܕ ܛܒ ܘܕܗܿܝ ܕܓܡܝܪܐ ܢܐܡܪ. ܒܪܬܚܐ ܕܚܘܒܗ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܡܘܟܟܗ ܘܛܢܢܗ ܕܒܕܡܘܬܗ ܕܗܽܘ ܪܡܐ ܕܒܡܘܟܟܗ. ܐܠܦ ܛܘܦܣܗ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ. ܗܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܛܢܢܐ. ܡܢܗ ܕܫܘܪܝܐ ܕܬܘܠܡܕܗ. ܓܒܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܦܠܘܚ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܨܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܫܚܬܐ ܕܥܘܡܪܗ. ܘܥܡ ܗܕܐ ܣܡ ܒܢܦܫܗ. ܕܠܐ ܢܚܙܐ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܕܥܐܠ ܠܥܘܡܪܐ. ܕܠܐ ܢܫܬܘܬܦ ܒܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܝܨܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ. ܘܡܟܝܠ. ܒܗܢܐ ܪܗܛܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܠܗ ܐܡܝܢ ܗܘܐ. ܟܕ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܘܬܐ. ܕܬܓܪܐ ܣܓܝ ܨܒܘ̈ܐܐ ܕܬܐܓܘܪܬܐ. ܕܐܦܢ ܡܢ ܚܕܐ ܬܒܕܛܠ ܬܐܓܘܪܬܗ. ܠܐ̱ܚܪܬܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܢܦܩܬܐ. ܕܢܫܬܟܚ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܥܠܠܬܐ ܕܝܘܬܪܢܐ. ܒܚܕ ܕܠܐ ܢܚܙܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܣܟܢܐ ܕܨܐܥ ܠܬܪܥܐ ܕܥܘܡܪܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܫܬܘܬܦ ܒܣܢܝܩܘܬܗ ܐܦܢ ܒܡܕܡ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܢܚܙܐ ܟܪܝܗܐ ܡܣܟܢܐ
which is called that of the Edessenes and | was also in intercourse with him for a space of some years, and during the days of my association with him | was with my whole soul thoroughly amazed at his keen zeal and his strenuous course of life, and his constant energy and his vigilance in every good work, and (that we may use an expression which sums up all) in the fervour of his love toward God, and his humility and his zeal that imitated that high one who by his humility taught the pattern (τύπος) of it to his disciples.
